# PANDORA,
->That is/<-

## The Noblest Gift of God,
->or the Worthy and Healing<-
->Philosophers’ Stone <note>original: "Stein der Weisen"</note>, with which the an-<-
->cient Philosophers, as well as Theophrastus Para-<-
->celsus, improved the imperfect metals through the<-
->power of fire: and likewise drove away all<-
->manner of harmful and incurable dis-<-
->eases, both internally and ex-<-
->ternally.<-

->was rescued from its destruction<-
->by a lover of this art,<-

->for the benefit of all people, especially the<-

The page features various ownership marks, including a royal Prussian stamp and a Chinese collector's seal, tracing its journey through history.</summary>

# Preface.

foreign words, which are all known to the **sons of wisdom** <note>original Latin: *filiis Sapientiæ*; a traditional term for initiates into the alchemical or hermetic mysteries.</note>, who do not let themselves be frightened by names like **Adarnech, Anfaha, Catrobil, Canze, Zingifur**, and so on. <note>These are "Decknamen" or cover-names—esoteric terms, often derived from Arabic or corrupted Greek, used to hide the identity of chemical substances from the uninitiated.</note>

However, because this art must go begging in these current times, and is held in great contempt by almost everyone because of **Theophrastus** <note>Theophrastus von Hohenheim, better known as Paracelsus (1493–1541).</note> (whom the philosophers rightly call the **Light of Nature** <note>original Latin: *Lumen Naturæ*; the divine wisdom implanted in the physical world that can be "read" by a true physician or alchemist.</note>), I have—at the request and advice of the **printer** <note>original Latin: *Typographi*</note>—had to seek another faithful **Epimetheus** <note>Epimetheus was the brother of Prometheus in Greek mythology; here, it refers to the editor/author's pseudonym "Franciscus Epimetheus."</note> for this **Pandora**. This "Pandora," beneath her beautiful form, makes fools of many who do not know how to approach her correctly. I sought one who would know how to deal with her so that **Jason** might obtain the **Golden Fleece** <note>original Latin: *aureum uellus*; a classical myth commonly used by alchemists as an allegory for the successful creation of the Philosopher's Stone.</note>.

Among all of them, however—Honorable and Highly Learned **Mr. Doctor Ruland** <note>Martin Ruland the Elder (1532–1602), a prominent physician and advocate of Paracelsian medicine.</note>, especially most gracious Lord—

# Preface.

and friendly receive her, and be her **Epimetheus** <note>In Greek mythology, Epimetheus was the husband of Pandora. The author is asking his patron to protect the book (Pandora) as a husband would a wife.</note>: so that she does not fall into the den of murderers <note>A common 16th-century idiom (German: *Mördergrube*) used to describe a world full of treachery and deceit.</note>, in this time, when almost no trust remains, even among close friends. I also further ask most highly of the Lord <note>Referring to his patron, Dr. Martin Ruland.</note>, that he would want to further promote me and my **medical** studies <note>original: *studia Medica*</note>, just as he has done until now.

If the Lord does this (as I indeed have no doubt he will), he shall find me indebted to him for the rest of my life. I wish herewith to have faithfully commended the Lord to Divine protection. In Basel, 1 September, Year 1582.

# PANDORA.

## The Book called / The most pre-
## cious Gift of God / made by
## Franciscus Epimetheus. <note>Franciscus Epimetheus is likely a pseudonym. In Greek mythology, Epimetheus ("afterthought") was the brother of Prometheus and the husband of Pandora. By using this name, the author positions himself as the guardian or "husband" of the wisdom contained in this book.</note>

Those who desire to have a true knowledge of the **philosophical art** <note>The "philosophical art" is a standard 16th-century term for Alchemy, the study of transforming matter and achieving spiritual perfection.</note> of greater wisdom should diligently examine this book and read it through often; so shall they achieve a fortunate outcome. Hear this, you sons and children of the ancient philosophers; I long to cry out with as loud, clear, and high a voice as I ever can. For I come to open the noble state of human things and the most secret treasure of all secrets <note>original: *heimligkeiten*. This phrasing evokes the "Secret of Secrets" (*Secretum Secretorum*), a famous medieval treatise on worldly and spiritual governance.</note> of the entire world; I wish to reveal this same treasure not through fiction, nor in a laughable or mocking way, but in the most certain and human manner. Therefore, devote yourselves with such diligence and earnestness...

<summary>This page serves as the title and opening address for "Pandora," an alchemical treatise. The author, writing under the pseudonym Franciscus Epimetheus, calls upon students of alchemy to study the text to uncover the ultimate secrets of the world.</summary>

<keywords>Pandora, Philosophical Art, Alchemy, Franciscus Epimetheus, Secret of Secrets, Hermetic wisdom, alchemical treasure</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the opening address of "Pandora" by the author Franciscus Epimetheus. He contrasts his own practical experience with the confusing writings of ancient philosophers and the failures of fraudulent alchemists.</meta>

-># The Gift of God.<-

...to listen and to pay heed, as I wish to bring to you a **mastery** <note>original: *Meisterschafft* — refers to both the skill of a master craftsman and the authoritative body of teaching.</note> of the teaching; namely, of those things which I have seen with my own eyes and touched with my own hands. Of this, I wish to demonstrate to you a more correct, certain, and intimate knowledge than that of the deceptive swindlers and boasters <note>original: *auffzöler vnd auffzieher* — derogatory terms for "puffers" or fraudulent alchemists who made grand claims but produced no gold.</note> who, after much expense and great labor, achieve nothing except that they finally fall into ruin and poverty. Therefore, I wish to speak clearly and openly with you, so that both the experienced and the inexperienced may understand through this mastery. 

Nor shall anyone be able to justly slander or speak ill of me; for the ancient **philosophers** <note>In this context, "philosophers" refers to the legendary alchemical authorities like Hermes Trismegistus, Geber, or Arnaldus de Villa Nova.</note> have written about these matters so obscurely and confusingly that they are not only misunderstood, but also serve no purpose at all. For this reason, those who wished to learn and investigate this precious **art** <note>The "Art" is alchemy, the study of the transmutation of base metals into noble ones and the perfection of the human soul.</note> were either deceived and cheated, or turned away from their purpose...

<summary>In this preface, the author claims his alchemical knowledge is based on direct physical experience rather than the intentionally obscure writings of the ancients or the failed experiments of "puffers." He promises a clear and honest explanation of the "Art" for both beginners and experts.</summary>

<keywords>The Gift of God, mastery, practical alchemy, ancient philosophers, alchemical fraud, the Art, sensory experience</keywords>

-># The Gift of God.<-

...lost their way therein, fell away, and wandered far from it.

I will, however, (putting aside all deception and concealment) set before your eyes the most truthful experience in its entirety, adding the opinions of the **philosophers** <note>original: *Philosophen* — specifically the alchemical masters of antiquity like Hermes Trismegistus or Geber, whose writings were often intentionally obscure.</note>, which best serves the right purpose, so that this matter of which we treat may be understood more openly and clearly.

Therefore, we first make it known that all those who work outside of **nature** <note>original: *Natur* — a core alchemical principle that "Art" (alchemy) must follow the natural laws of growth and transformation rather than trying to force impossible results.</note> are deceivers, and act and labor in an unseemly matter. Furthermore, nothing is born of a human being but a human; of an animal, an animal; and each like brings forth its like; therefore, that which does not possess its own essence within it cannot produce something foreign according to its own pleasure or likeness. We say this so that no one may lose their money; for some, deceived by the slowness of their **understanding** <note>original: *Verstand* — here referring to a lack of intellectual or spiritual discernment to see through the tricks of fraudulent alchemists.</note>...

# The Gift of God.

...become and fall into poverty. These same people should also not lead others astray, nor drive and bring them into poverty and wretchedness. I advise, however, that no one should let themselves be drawn into trying to find this **Art** <note>original: *Kunſt* — the "Great Work" of alchemy.</note>, for it has made fools of many and led them into great folly. This discovery does not require many things, but only one thing. 

<margin>A red vertical line in the margin highlights the following section emphasizing the simplicity of the true work.</margin>
It also demands no great expense, for it is only one **stone** <term>Philosopher's Stone — the legendary substance that alchemists believed could transmute metals and grant long life.</term>, one medicine, one vessel, one **regimen** <note>original: *regiment* — the sequence of heating and cooling used to "rule" the substance in the flask.</note>, and one order; and know that it is a truthful and the most truthful art. 

Also, the **philosophers** <note>The ancient masters of alchemy, such as Hermes or Geber.</note> would never have been so diligent in describing so many and varied types and sequences of colors if they had not seen, touched, or felt them themselves. Therefore, we reiterate this: all those who work and labor outside of **nature** <note>The principle that alchemy must follow natural laws, like a seed growing in soil, rather than using unnatural chemicals or methods.</note> are themselves deceived and are also deceivers. Therefore, let your practice be in nature and in diligent service. For our Stone is made of a soulful, sustaining, and mineral—

# The Gift of God.
<note>original: "Die Gab Gottes." A reference to the *Donum Dei*, a famous alchemical treatise.</note>

We shall go and seek the natures of the four elements, these being formally derived from the Book of the Earth.

You have asked how many true colors there are, and I will very gladly tell you: Know that there are three right, perfect colors, from which all other colors take their beginning. The first is black, the second white, the third red. <note>These represent the three main stages of the alchemical process: *Nigredo* (blackening/death), *Albedo* (whitening/purification), and *Rubedo* (reddening/perfection).</note> There are many other colors, but these are not to be regarded or worried about, for they often vanish before knowledge. <note>The author refers to "intermediate" colors, often called the "Peacock’s Tail," which appear briefly during the process but are not stable goals.</note>

In that same place occurs a joining together of two bodies, and it is necessary in our Mastery; <term>Mastery</term> <note>original: *Meisterschafft*; refers to the "Great Work" or the art of alchemy itself.</note> and if one of these bodies were alone in our Stone, it would never in any way provide a <term>tincture</term> <note>original: *tinctur*; in alchemy, a tincture is a substance capable of "staining" or transforming the inner nature of a base metal into gold.</note> or coloring. And therefore the joining together of these two is necessary; which two, once they are joined and accepted **are in the joining together of the Stone, which is conceived in the belly of the wind; and that is what the Philosopher says: The wind has carried it.** <note>original: "Es hat jn getragē der wind." This is a direct reference to the *Tabula Smaragdina* (Emerald Tablet) of Hermes Trismegistus, a foundational alchemical text which states, "The wind carried it in its belly."</note>

# The Raven’s Head
<note>original: *Das Haupt des Rappen*. Also known by the Latin term *Caput Corvi*, this represents the *Nigredo* or "blackening" phase, the first essential stage of the Great Work, signifying the death and purification of the matter.</note>

<image-desc>A woodcut illustration of a glass laboratory flask with a rounded body and narrow neck. At the bottom of the flask, two serpentine or dragon-like worms are depicted in a circular struggle, with one biting the tail or body of the other, symbolizing the alchemical process of putrefaction.</image-desc>

The black and dreg-filled Earth of the Philosophers <note>The "Philosophers" refers to the alchemical authorities of the past; "Earth" here refers to the solid precipitate or residue.</note>.

Worms are created, among which one eats or devours the other.

The breaking or destruction of one is the birth of another <note>This is an English translation of the common alchemical maxim *corruptio unius est generatio alterius*, suggesting that the death of the base metal is necessary for the birth of the gold.</note>.

Now this earth lies at the bottom of the vessel and is completely dissolved <note>original: *dissoluiert*</note> or released into a water, as was mentioned before.

--- Page 60 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the "Nigredo" or blackening phase, specifically addressing the timing and signs of the Stone’s dissolution and the subsequent purification of the material.</meta>

->The Gift of God.<-

Here it is asked: in how much time is the Stone <note>In alchemy, "the Stone" refers to the Philosophers' Stone in its various stages of development; here, it is the raw material undergoing its first transformation.</note> turned into blackness, and what is the sign of the true solution <note>original: *solution*; the process of a solid substance dissolving into a liquid state.</note> of the Stone? I give as an answer: When the blackness has appeared for the first time, it is a sign of the putrefaction <note>original: *putrefaction*; the "rotting" or breaking down of the matter, considered a necessary death before rebirth.</note> or rotting, and the solution or dissolution of the Stone. But when the blackness completely fades and passes away, that is a sign of the complete rotting of the Stone and its dissolution.

Likewise, it is asked whether the black mists remain upon the aforementioned Stone for forty days <note>original: *xl. tag*. The number 40 is highly symbolic in alchemy, mirroring the forty days of the Great Flood or forty days in the wilderness.</note>. I answer thus: Sometimes more, sometimes less; this variation occurs, however, because of the different quantities of the medicine <note>original: *artzney*; alchemists often referred to their substance as a "medicine" that could heal "sick" (base) metals into "healthy" (noble) gold.</note>, and it is also affected by the diligence of the work; therefore, a greater quantity requires more time, and a smaller quantity requires less time. The wisdom of the operation helps in the diligent separation <note>original: *sünderung*; refers to the purging of impurities from the matter.</note> of the blackness.

Likewise, it is asked how long this rotting and purification of the Earth <note>The "Earth" refers to the solid precipitate or "body" of the matter at the bottom of the vessel.</note> will last. I answer: forty days.

<summary>This page discusses the timeline and visual indicators of the Nigredo stage, explaining that the appearance and eventual disappearance of blackness signify the successful rotting and dissolution of the alchemical matter.</summary>

# The Gift of God <note>original: *Die Gab Gottes*; the German translation of the Latin *Donum Dei*.</note>

Water and fire are sufficient for you, for they wash, cleanse, and renew—or preserve—the **body** <note>original: *corpus*; the physical substance within the alchemical vessel.</note> and take away its darkness; for the water that dwells in the **Air** <note>In alchemy, air represents the volatile, spiritual state.</note> reaches for the **Earth** <note>The fixed, physical state.</note> just as iron reaches for the magnet.

Therefore, repeat all these procedures over it four times, and finally **calcine** <note>To heat a substance to high temperatures to reduce it to a powder.</note> it according to the custom that fixes and calcines; then you have governed this most precious **Stone** <note>The Philosopher's Stone, the goal of the Great Work.</note> with sufficient care.

**Calcination** <term>Calcination: The process of reducing a solid to ash or powder through prolonged heating, intended to "kill" the old form so a new one can be born.</term>—is nothing other than drying out and turning into ash; therefore, it should be burned without fear until it becomes ash. When it has become ash, you have mixed it well. You should not despise or disregard this same ash, but rather give back to it the **sweat** <note>original: *schweiß*; refers to the distilled liquid or "soul" of the matter that was separated earlier in the process.</note> that it had cast out.

Therefore, once all the water is drawn out and turned into earth, let it be **putrefied** <note>original: *gefeult*; the stage of *Putrefactio*, often called the "death of the matter," where it turns black and decomposes before its final purification.</note> or made to rot in its vessel for several days over a light fire...

<meta>catchword: chet/ (likely the start of "geschieht" or "geschirr" on the following page)</meta>

<summary>This page instructs the alchemist to use water and fire to cleanse the "body" of the Stone, repeating the process four times. It defines calcination as the reduction of the matter to ash and emphasizes the importance of reuniting this ash with its previously distilled essence ("sweat").</summary>

...[cook] it <note>The text begins with the suffix of the word "kochet" (cooks).</note> until the precious white color springs forth on top.

# The Gift of God <note>original: *Die Gab Gottes*; the German translation of the Latin *Donum Dei*, a famous 15th-century alchemical treatise.</note>

Just as the year is divided into four parts, so too is our **blessed work**. The first part is the cold and moist **Winter**, which is rainy. The second is **Spring** <note>original: *Glentz*; an archaic German term for the spring season or the period of Lent.</note>, which is warm, moist, and flowering. The third is **Summer**, a dry and warm time, and **Red**. The fourth is **Autumn**, cold and dry, the time to gather the fruit. With this same order, govern the **tincturing** <note>original: *tingierenden*; from the Latin *tingere*, referring to the substances that have the power to "color" or transmute base metals.</note> or coloring natures, until they bring forth seasonal and ripe fruit, according to the verse or saying:

"The winter is past, the rain is over and gone, and has departed; for the flowers have appeared in our earth in the time of Spring." <note>This is a paraphrase of the Song of Solomon 2:11-12, a biblical text frequently used by alchemists to describe the "blooming" of the Stone after the "winter" of purification.</note>

But we have rested upon the **white rose**, for they bring—or say—that every **body** <note>original: *cörper*; in alchemy, "bodies" refers to the seven known metals (Gold, Silver, Mercury, Copper, Iron, Tin, and Lead).</note> is difficult to transform into true **Silver**.

Therefore, when you see this whiteness appearing, leading the way before all else, then be certain that in this whiteness, a **redness** is hidden. Then it is not necessary to extract that same whiteness; instead, continue to **cook** <note>original: *kochen*; the process of "decoction" or heating the matter in a sealed vessel over a long period.</note> it until it becomes completely and entirely red.

<summary>This page uses the metaphor of the four seasons to describe the progression of the Great Work, culminating in the transition from the white stage (Silver) to the final red stage (Gold).</summary>

# The Gift of God.

## Geber in his *Summa*, in the sixteenth
### Chapter, on the natural beginnings.

For it is to be noted that after the **Stone** <term>Stone</term> <note>The Philosopher's Stone, the legendary substance capable of transmuting base metals into gold and silver.</note> has been purified and perfectly cleansed of all corrupting or destructive things, and afterward **fermented** or **leavened** <note>original: "fermentiert oder geheblet." Similar to how yeast leavens bread, alchemists added a "ferment" to their mixture to stabilize it and direct its final transformation.</note>, you do not need to change the **vessel**, nor to open it, but only—may God preserve it—that it may not be broken. And for this reason, the **Philosophers** <note>A term used for the alchemical authorities of the past, such as Geber, Hermes, or Aristotle.</note> have said that the entire **Mastery** <term>Mastery</term> <note>original: "Meisterschafft," referring to the *Magnum Opus* or the Great Work of alchemy.</note> now takes place in a single vessel.

For in its purification, it cannot have a certain, fixed time, but only according to how correctly and well the practitioner or worker labors. Here I teach you a beautiful lesson and true foundation, how one shall arrive at the end of the **Philosophers' Stone** <term>Philosophers' Stone</term> <note>The ultimate goal of alchemy, believed to be a substance that could transmute base metals into gold and act as a universal medicine.</note>—whether it be for gold or for silver—which is called a **tincture** <term>tincture</term> <note>A potent liquid or powder capable of "staining" or transforming the properties of a metal.</note>, or medicine, or **Elixir** <term>Elixir</term>. By the **Mercury** <note>original: "Quecksilber"</note> **sublimated** with **Salt**, **Alum**, **Vitriol**, and the like, you should understand this: that one should take Mercury to the Gold and to the Silver and sublimate them together. # The Gift of God.

**-my, that the species of metallic things cannot be changed.** <note>original Latin: "Alchymiae, Species rerum metallicarum permutari non posse." This is a famous quote from the "Secreta Secretorum" or other pseudo-Aristotelian texts that challenged alchemists: it suggests that while you can make things look like gold, you cannot change their essential nature.</note> This is to say: the practitioners of the art—those who wish to work by artifice differently than nature works within the earth to make gold or silver, or to transform one metal into another—should know that the <term>species</term> <note>The "species" refers to the essential, God-given category or "kind" of a thing which, in traditional philosophy, was thought to be immutable.</note> of metals cannot be transformed one into another. They cannot change the species of silver into the species (that is, the property) of gold, nor the properties of copper, lead, tin, or iron into the properties of gold or silver.

And that is true; however, only the **form** <note>original: "gestalt." While the "species" (essence) was fixed, the "form" (the way that essence is expressed physically) was thought to be malleable by alchemists.</note> of one metal may be transformed into the form of another metal. Understand it this way: the form of silver may indeed be transformed into the form of gold. The master of nature, **Aristotle**, does not speak against this, for all metals have one origin and one **seed** <note>original: "sperm," used here as the generative biological principle applied to minerals.</note> from which they come, and that is **Mercury**. Therefore, all metals may indeed be brought back again into Mercury.

<summary>This page addresses a famous philosophical objection to alchemy—that one "species" cannot become another—and resolves it by arguing that while the essence remains, the "form" of metals can be changed because they all share Mercury as their original "seed."</summary>

<keywords>Aristotle, Alchemy, Species vs Form, Mercury, First Matter, Transformation, Gold, Silver, Metallurgy</keywords>

## That Mercury is a Material for All Metals.

For Aristotle <note>original: "Aristoteles." For they [gold and silver] cannot accomplish this entirely on their own; and if they were to give some of their perfection to another, they would then remain imperfect, because they cannot <term>tinge: (German: *tingieren*) To dye or deeply penetrate a substance with a new quality or color; to transmute.</term> or dye more than their own extent. # What the first work of the Philosophers is, and how the same should be understood, the practitioners of Alchemy should know.

**T**he work of the Philosophers is to **dissolve** <note>original: *dissoluieren*. This matter is, in the first place, **Mercury**, since it is an element of all generative things <note>original: *zeügigen dingen*. Substances capable of growth or transformation.</note>. <meta>This page continues the technical discussion of elemental transformation from page 113. The previous page concluded by explaining that elements without shared qualities (like fire and water) cannot transform into each other directly, but must instead be mediated through a third state. This page begins by identifying Fire's relationship to the Earth and introduces the four core operations required for the alchemical "Mastery."</meta>

-> ### The Gift of God. ### <-

...fire <note>The previous page ended with "then one gains the," leading into this page's first word, "fire."</note> has communion with the Earth in its dryness; and for that reason, when the dryness is turned into cold, into warmth, or into heat, and the heat into dryness, then you have and gain the entire <term>Mastery</term> <note>German: *Meisterschafft*. In alchemy, this refers to the successful completion of the Great Work or the production of the Philosopher's Stone.</note>. However, the way you should govern this—how to turn and transform one <term>element</term> <note>The four classical elements: Fire, Air, Water, and Earth.</note> into the other—consists of 4 things specifically through which this happens. The first is <term>solution</term> <note>Original: *soluierung* (from Latin *solutio*). The process of dissolving a solid into a liquid state.</note>; the second is washing; the third is <term>reduction</term> <note>Original: *reducierung* (from Latin *reductio*). Here meaning the restoration of a substance to a previous or more essential state.</note> (that is, "bringing back"); the 4th is <term>fixation</term> <note>Original: *fixierung*. Making a volatile substance stable so that it can withstand fire without evaporating.</note>. 

<term>Solution</term> is when one makes the coarse <term>subtle</term> <note>German: *subtil*. Refers to a substance being refined, purified, or rendered into a more spiritual/ethereal form.</note> and simple. Washing is when one makes the black clear and white. <term>Reduction</term> is when one makes the moist dry. <term>Fixation</term> is when one makes the volatile stable and dry. 

<term>Solution</term> is the breaking and dividing, and the making of the <term>first matter</term> <note>German: *erste materien* (Latin: *prima materia*). The primitive, formless base of all matter which alchemists sought to isolate before rebuilding it into gold.</note>. Washing is when one makes it moist, distills, and <term>calcines</term> <note>Original: *calcinier*. To heat a substance to high temperatures in order to reduce it to a powder or ash.</note> it. <term>Reduction</term> is the restoration: so that it is oily like butter and flows like wax and becomes <term>subtle</term>. <term>Fixation</term>

## Mercury.

Father, I am a father of all metals; and since I am a father, and a slimy water, and a sulfurous earth, I am therefore in the depths of the earth, so that I may have no exit, for I have been joined together...

## Thomas Aquinas ##
<note>While this text is attributed to the famous theologian Thomas Aquinas (1225–1274), historians generally consider such alchemical works to be "pseudo-Aquinas"—written by later authors using his name to lend authority to their secrets.</note>

O you most beloved brother! By your diligent request, which you have made for a long time, you have prevailed upon me to [write] a short treatise concerning our <term>Art</term> <note>The "Art" refers to **alchemy**, the ancient practice of chemical transformation and the search for the Philosopher's Stone.</note>—a certain rule, a shining operation, a useful <term>tincture</term> <note>In alchemy, a "tincture" is a concentrated substance capable of "staining" or transforming the internal properties of a base metal into gold or silver.</note>. And truly I will reveal [it] to you and divide it into eight chapters, and I wish to ask three things of you:

Firstly, that you do not pay much heed to the many different words of the <term>philosophers</term> <note>In this context, "philosophers" refers to earlier alchemical authors who often used confusing or contradictory language.</note> who write or speak about this Art; for the Art consists only in comprehension and in understanding, and in the demonstration of the operation. There the Art has established its throne. And truly, the philosophers wished to conceal the Art and the truth, and did so for the sake of the unworthy; and they spoke all of it *figurativè* <note>Original Latin: *figurativè*. Meaning "figuratively" or symbolically.</note>, that is, figuratively and secretively.

Secondly, I ask you to guard against many things—namely, buying various <term>species</term> <note>In early chemistry, "species" refers to specific raw materials, chemicals, or minerals used in experiments.</note> and employing them, for
<meta>catchword: because / why</meta>

### The Gift of God <note>original: "Die Gottes Gab." This is the German title for the *Donum Dei*, a famous alchemical treatise attributed to various authors including Arnold of Villanova or George Anathas.</note>

Through the **Raven** <note>original: "per Coruum." In alchemical symbolism, the Raven represents *nigredo* or the "blackness," a stage of decay and purification where the matter is broken down before it can be reborn.</note>, lay it in a **sublimatory** <note>original: "sublimatorium." A specialized vessel used to heat a solid until it turns into vapor and then condenses back into a solid on a cooler surface, leaving impurities behind.</note> / and without any addition proceed to sublimate it / For then you have your **mercury** <note>original: "Mercurium"</note> well-prepared for this **Art** / pure and clear as a mirror and crystal.

This is now the true beginning of this **Art** / for all metals have their origin in **mercury**. Therefore, if one wishes to turn them into **the Sun** <note>original: "Solem." The alchemical name for gold.</note> / that is / Gold / or into **the Moon** <note>original: "Lunam." The alchemical name for silver.</note> / that is Silver / then a **medicine** <note>the alchemical "elixir" or "tincture" used to transform metals.</note> must first be made from **the Sun** or **the Moon** / as if from their origin / which is **mercury**. And this is done so that they may be brought back again / into their first essence and matter / that is / into **mercury**. This, however, cannot happen in any other way / except solely with the **mercury** / which is approved and added to **the Sun** and **the Moon**. When this happens in such a way / then the **common mercury** <note>original: "comunis Mercurius." The standard mercury found in nature, as opposed to the "philosophical mercury" sought by alchemists.</note> which one calls the **Spirit** <note>original: "Spiritum"</note> / [causes] **the Sun** and **the Moon** to turn themselves into their own likeness, into **mercury** / which one calls [the Mercury] **of Metals** <note>original: "Metallorum." The text breaks off here.</note>

# The Gift of God

->I iiiij<-

...or the **Mercury of the Philosophers** <note>original: "Mercurium Philosophorum." In alchemy, this refers to a purified, "living" essence of mercury, distinct from common quicksilver, which serves as the base for the Philosopher's Stone.</note>. For it is said among the philosophers, **Nature rejoices in nature** <note>original: "Natura natura lætatur." A famous alchemical axiom suggesting that substances of a similar essence naturally combine and perfect one another.</note>; that is, one nature delights in the other. This should be understood in this way: the nature of a man rejoices in the nature of, or with the nature of, the woman.

Therefore Aristotle, the master of nature, says: **Things are not made except according to their natures, as a human from a human, a lion from a lion** <note>original: "Res non fiunt nisi secundùm naturas eorum, ut homo de homine, Leo de Leone." This principle of "homogeneous" generation was used by alchemists to argue that gold can only be grown from a "seed" of gold or its natural root.</note>. This means: any thing, or any kind of thing, is produced according to its nature. Just as a human is made and born only from a human, a lion from a lion, and the like. For every nature in particular rejoices in its own nature, which is its very own essence.

Just as all humans descend from Adam and Eve; Adam’s nature rejoices in Eve’s nature, because Eve was created from Adam. Therefore they have joy with one another and rejoice together, and their **seed** <note>original: "Saam." In an alchemical context, "seed" refers to the generative power or "sulfur" within a substance that allows it to multiply and transform.</note> is multiplied, and that which came from both of them has been multiplied—countless numbers of people.

<summary>This page explains the alchemical principle of affinity and generation, arguing that mercury must be refined into its 'philosophical' state to act as the true seed of metals, just as humans and animals reproduce according to their own nature.</summary>
<keywords>Mercury of the Philosophers, Aristotle, nature rejoices in nature, Adam and Eve, alchemical generation, multiplication, seed of metals</keywords>

# The Gift of God

Just as the *Raven* <note>original: "Rapp." In alchemy, the raven is a traditional symbol for the "Nigredo" stage—the first phase of the Great Work, characterized by blackness, decay, and the "death" of the old form.</note> is black by nature, and the blackness does not leave it, so this blackness cannot be removed by any washing, except through *sublimation* <term>Sublimation|The alchemical process of heating a substance until it turns into vapor and then solidifies again. It was seen as a way to "spiritualize" or purify the material by lifting it away from its dregs.</term> alone. Therefore the Masters speak thus: *putrefaction is the corruption of matter, and the corruption of one is the generation of another* <note>original Latin: "putrefactio est corruptio materiæ, & corruptio unius est generatio alterius." This is a fundamental alchemical principle: the old form must "die" or decay before a new, higher form can be born.</note>. That is to say: the *putrefaction* <note>original: "fäulung"</note> of a thing or a matter is a *breaking down* <note>original: "zerbrechligkeit," literally "fragility" or "breakability"</note> of that same matter, and this same breaking down is a greening and a growth, and a restoration of the matter; so that when the same matter has decayed, it thereafter grows and becomes green, and from one part, a hundred parts might grow. I give you an analogy: a farmer who sows a seed in a field, and it decays and breaks, so that thereafter it no longer resembles a seed; afterwards, when the *Summer* comes, that same seed begins to turn green, and returns to its former essence—that is, into a little grain—and grows in multiplication and in all *powers* <note>original: "Krefften," referring to the inherent medicinal or transformative virtues of the substance.</note>.

<summary>This page explains the necessity of the "blackening" stage (putrefaction), arguing that the decay of the material is the essential prerequisite for its rebirth and multiplication. It uses the natural analogy of a seed rotting in the earth before it can grow into a new plant.</summary>
<keywords>Nigredo, Raven, Putrefaction, Sublimation, Corruption, Generation, Analogy of the Seed, Multiplication</keywords>

<meta>This page follows the summary of the three pillars of alchemy (Matter, Art, and Fire). The author now transitions from these theoretical definitions into the "Practica" or the actual physical procedure, emphasizing the "Unity" of the process—one matter, one vessel, and one furnace.</meta>

-># The Gift of God<-

Therefore, we shall now descend to the **practice** and procedure, and submit ourselves to them clearly, openly, and understandably. Therefore, take note that in the whole **Art** <term>Art (Kunst)|The "Great Work" of alchemy, seen as a combination of laboratory science and spiritual discipline.</term> and our operation—which is more Divine than human—only a single **matter** <term>Matter (materi)|The raw starting substance, which alchemists believed contained the "seed" of gold.</term> is required, and absolutely no other.

For our water is pure and **mineral-natured** <note>original: "Erzäderlich." You may prepare this first degree of the fire in two ways. One way is to place a great quantity of ashes in the place where the fire is to be lit. In these ashes, however, a pit, hole, or hollow should be made. Then, take a scale and weigh the coals, taking 12 ounces <note>The symbol "z" in the manuscript denotes an *uncia* or ounce, a standard unit of measure for alchemical ingredients and fuel.</note> of them. This said eye should look straight and directly up at the bottom of the vessel on the hearth in which the material <note>original: *materi*. This is the chemical substance undergoing transformation.</note> is held.

# The Gift of God.

<term>Albation</term> <note>original: *albation* or *weißmachung*; from the Latin *albus*, meaning white. This refers to the *Albedo* stage of the alchemical Great Work, where the blackened matter is purified until it shines like silver.</note>, or making white, is accomplished and fulfilled by the fire of the second degree within one hundred days. The fire of the second degree, however, is contained within this verse or rhyme:

> *Nature rejoices in the second degree when the senses are balanced,* <note>original Latin: *Sensibus æquatis gaudet natura secundo*</note>

In German, it sounds like this: When the senses are made equal, nature desires the second. It might well be more clearly rhymed as follows:

->So now the senses are well balanced,<-
->The second fire shall gladden nature.<-

## The first <term>investigation</term> <note>original: *indago* / *erforschung*; a systematic inquiry or search.</note>, namely of our Heaven, or the <term>quintessence</term> <note>original: *fünfftē wesens*; literally the "fifth essence," a pure, incorruptible substance distinct from the four earthly elements.</note>.

To seek the hidden thing, which possesses a form relative to the four qualities or <term>complexions</term> <note>original: *complexen*; in medieval science, this refers to the balance of the four humors (blood, phlegm, yellow bile, and black bile) that make up the human body.</note> from which our body is composed, just as the Heaven possesses a form relative to the four elements. The <term>Philosophers</term> <note>Referring to ancient natural philosophers and alchemists.</note> have called the Heaven the fifth essence, in consideration of the four elements and as the fifth following those same four; for the Heaven, for itself and in itself, is unbreakable, indestructible, and unchangeable, and is a thing that takes on no foreign impressions, unless it happens by the command of God. Thus also the thing we seek, in consideration of the four elements of our body, should be as the fifth essence: in itself unbreakable, even if it were to stand for all eternity; neither hot and dry with fire, nor cold and moist with water, nor warm and moist with air, nor cold and dry with earth; rather, it is the fifth essence, powerful against the opposing <insert>forces</insert> <note>original: *widertheil*; the conflicting qualities of the physical elements.</note>

196

The Gift of God.

## The art and the knowledge to extract the quintessence from quicksilver and Roman vitriol. ##

The Almighty and highest God has so ordained it, that the **fifth essence** <note>original: "fünfft wesē" or "quintam essentiam"; the purest, most concentrated essence of a substance</note> of vitriol and of **copperas** <note>original: "cuperrose"; a term for metallic sulfates, usually green vitriol or iron sulfate</note> should be extracted invisibly with **quicksilver** <note>mercury</note>; for when it is **sublimated** <note>term: "sublimated" — a chemical process where a solid is heated into a vapor and then collected as a purified solid; in alchemy, this was seen as "spiritualizing" the matter</note> and elevated with these and with common salt, then that same quicksilver, rising in the whitest form, undoubtedly draws with it the quintessence of the vitriol and the copperas. And then you have sublimated quicksilver with these two, and you have invisibly hidden their quintessence within the whiteness of the aforementioned quicksilver. This invisible quintessence, or fifth essence, is called **the sulfur of the philosophers** <note>original: "sulphur Philosophorū"</note>, which is invisible; therefore they say that their sulfur is not the sulfur of the common people, namely common sulfur. ## The art of reducing and reintroducing the sublimated mercury or its quintessence, separated, into the water which is called by the philosophers *Lac Virginum* or "Virgin’s Milk." <note>original: "Lac Virginum oder Jungfrawen Milch"</note>

<meta>The previous page concluded the work of John of Rupescissa on the quintessence. This page begins a new section titled "The Gift of God," which shifts focus from distillation techniques to elective astrology—the practice of choosing the most auspicious times for alchemical operations based on the positions of the stars and planets.</meta>

->### The Gift of God.###<-

To know: If one wishes to fix <note>to stabilize a substance so it no longer evaporates or changes when heated</note> or whatever one wishes to work with fire, it is necessary to know in which sign the Moon's light resides; and especially fix and work with fire in **Aries**, in **Leo**, and in **Sagittarius**, in **Taurus**, **Capricorn**, and in **Virgo**.

One may also distill in these same signs.

**The Sun.** In **Aries** and in **Leo** it is good to work with **Gold**; it is bad in **Aquarius** and **Libra**.

**The Moon.** It is good to work with **Silver** when the Moon is in **Taurus** and in **Cancer**; and it is bad in **Scorpio** and in **Capricorn**.

**Saturn.** With **Lead** it is good to work in **Libra**, **Capricorn**, and **Aquarius**; and it is bad in **Aries**, **Cancer**, and **Leo**.

**Jupiter.** With **Tin** it is good to work when the Moon is in **Cancer**, **Pisces**, and **Sagittarius**; and it is bad in **Virgo** and **Gemini**.

With **Aes** <note>original: "es .i. Erz"; the Latin word *aes* (bronze or copper ore), followed by the abbreviation for *id est* (that is), and the German *Erz* (ore)</note>, that is, **Ore**, it is good to work in **Capricorn**, **Aries**, and **Scorpio**; and it is bad in **Cancer**, **Libra**, and **Taurus**.

**Venus.** Working with **Copper** is good in **Pisces**, **Libra**, **Taurus**, and **Virgo**; and it is bad in **Aries** and **Capricorn**.

**Mars.** With **Iron** it is good.

**Mercury.** With **Quicksilver** <note>mercury</note> it is good to work in **Virgo** and **Gemini**; and it is bad in **Sagittarius** and **Pisces**.

->The Gift of God.<-

# This saying belongs to the first
## Figure on the 210th
### leaf.

His power torments me, a naked woman,
For unhappy was my first body.
And never yet was a mother born,
Until I was born for the second time <note>This "second birth" refers to the alchemical process of sublimation or purification, where the matter is "reborn" in a higher state.</note>.
Then I gained the power of all herbs;
In every illness I became victorious.
Of my Son I took close heed,
And came there with him as one of two <note>original: "selb ander"; an archaic German expression meaning "together with one other." Here it suggests the union of the feminine and masculine principles.</note>.
When I became pregnant with him,
And gave birth on a barren field.
I became a mother and yet remained a maiden,
And was led into my true essence.
So that my Son became my Father,
As God has ordained in an essential way.
The mother who has borne me,
Through me she was born upon this Earth.
One thing to consider with natural wonder:
The birth has masterfully swallowed that up.
From this, four come into one <note>This refers to the four elements (Fire, Air, Water, Earth) being unified into the Quintessence.</note>,
In our <term>Masterful Stone</term> <note>The Philosopher's Stone, the goal of the alchemical "Great Work."</note>.
And seven considered in threefold,
And brought into one essence <note>The "seven" likely refers to the seven metals or planets, and "threefold" to the three alchemical principles: Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury.</note>.

He who can consider this very well,
To him the power is given,
So that he drives away all sickness,
From metals and from human bodies.
Without God’s help, no one may achieve <note>original: "gebawen"; literally "to build" or "to cultivate," here referring to the successful completion of the Great Work.</note> this,

From my nature springs a tree,

One stretches toward the East <note>original: "Orient."</note>,
And the other toward the West <note>original: "Occident."</note>.
From there <term>eagles</term> <note>In alchemy, eagles symbolize sublimation—the process where a solid is heated into a vapor (rises) and then condenses back into a solid (falls), purifying the substance.</note> fly and burn their plumage,

And are subject to the Sun and Moon <note>The Sun (Gold) and Moon (Silver) represent the masculine and feminine, or fixed and volatile, principles.</note>.

He has everything in His keeping.

Where all bodies and souls are joined exactly,
So that they soar in their Father’s Kingdom;
Thus the Art <term>Kunst</term> <note>Alchemy was frequently referred to simply as "The Art" or "The Sacred Art."</note> sustains itself on the Earth.

->A<-

->B<-

# A

If you wish to unlock the barred locks,

### The Gift of God:
A

The <term>Dragon</term><note>original: "Track". In alchemical symbolism, the dragon often represents the "First Matter" (materia prima) or crude Mercury that must be refined.</note> is the seed of man and woman, born out of them. When it feels the natural heat of the sun, it will spread and turn green, and swallow its own progenitor. From this there then springs a black mountain, streaked with red and sprinkled with white flowers, like a mountain in a green meadow. Upon it, yellow flowers wander with large and beautiful brown buds. Atop this sits a golden crown of pure <unclear>clarity</unclear><note>original: "Ihon". Whoever is truly present with it—in heart, mind, and spirit—and lifts it from the <term>glow</term><note>The intense heat of the alchemical furnace or "athanor".</note>, just as the <term>Philosophers</term><note>The "Philosophers" refers to the ancient alchemical authorities like Hermes Trismegistus or Geber.</note> write of it; through this, one may drive away all sickness and poverty, both from the bodies of metals and from the bodies of men.

The King and the Queen are very powerful; they cannot endure any over-burdening. Yield to them, for they give you their eyes for their head. -># The Gift of God<-

-come, and our Son rules and reigns now, and is arrayed in red armor <note>original: "Rotten waaffen"; signifies the *Rubedo* or Red stage, the final perfection in alchemy where the substance becomes "fixed" and powerful.</note>. Our Son, the born King, takes the <term>tincture</term> <note>A potent substance capable of transforming base metals into gold or healing illness.</note> from the fire; the sea, death, and darkness flee from him; and the **Dragon**, which haunts the hollow places, flees the stream of the **Sun**. From a royal father is born the mother-maiden, whereby nothing is lost; and out of three faces <note>Likely referring to the three alchemical principles: Sulfur, Mercury, and Salt.</note>, a single being is made, from which the **Raven** begins to stir and join itself into our blackness <note>The *Nigredo* stage of decay and fermentation.</note>. # The Gift of God.

The earth is a mother of the metals, and the heaven is the father of the same, according to the opinion of <term>Hermes<note>Refers to Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Hellenistic figure to whom many foundational alchemical texts were attributed.</note></term>; and the earth is even now pregnant with them in mountains, fields, plains, waters, and all other places.

<term>Mercury<note>original: "Mercurius"; here referring to the element quicksilver.</note></term> is found in the reeds and damp mossy grounds; for this, place iron pipes or channels with an earthenware tile or shard beneath them, and place or build a charcoal fire around it; thus the mercury will flow out from it. It is also often found in the secret chambers <note>This likely refers to latrines or privies, where mercury was sometimes recovered from medicinal or industrial waste.</note> or outhouses. Likewise, it is found in the clay of draw-wells and in the eaves of roofs.

Thus one shall roast <term>antimony<note>A semi-metal often used in alchemical purification processes.</note></term>: take as much of it as you wish and place it upon a glowing coal, and blow upon it with a bellows until it becomes glowing; and whatever has become glowing, remove it immediately from the fire, for if it remains in there too long, it would be turned into the nature of lead.

# The Gift of God.

Aristotle speaks concerning natures: the luster or the white is born out of blackness and <term>saffron-yellow<note>original: "saffergelben"; this refers to *citrinitas*, a stage in the alchemical process between the white and the red.</note></term>; the saffron-yellow color comes forth from the white and red. Now, if the white is separated from the black, the saffron-yellow remains left behind. Likewise, if the saffron-yellow is drawn away from the white or luster, the red will be left there; and if you take the saffron-yellow from the luster, you will find the black; if the red is removed from the beautiful white, the saffron-color will be left there. <note>This passage describes the interdependency of the four primary alchemical colors: *nigredo* (black), *albedo* (white), *citrinitas* (yellow), and *rubedo* (red).</note>

<term>Albertus Magnus<note>A 13th-century German Dominican friar and philosopher widely (though often apocryphally) credited with significant alchemical discoveries.</note></term> says that this is the best alchemical operation among all alchemical operations: that which comes forth from those things from which nature itself comes forth—such as the purification of <term>sulfur<note>original: "schwebel"; in alchemy, sulfur represents the combustible, active, and "masculine" principle of matter.</note></term> through <term>decoction<note>original: "abkochung oder absiedung"; the process of boiling a substance to extract its essence or purify it.</note></term> and <term>sublimation<note>The process of heating a solid until it becomes a vapor and then allowing it to solidify again, used for purification.</note></term>, and from the purification of <term>quicksilver<note>original: "Quecksilbers"; also known as mercury, representing the volatile, "feminine" principle.</note></term>. Through the proper mixing of these, every form of every metal is brought forth and extracted.

# The Gift of God.

But those who whiten through white things or yellow through yellow things—while the form of the original metal remains in the matter—they are deceivers, and they do not make true Gold and true Silver. <note>The author is warning against "sophistication," a practice where alchemists merely dyed the surface of a metal rather than achieving true transmutation of its essence.</note>

The heat that works in the moist first produces and brings forth blackness; and in the dry, whiteness; and in the white, yellowness. <note>This describes the progression of the "Great Work" through its color stages: *nigredo* (black), *albedo* (white), and *citrinitas* (yellow).</note>

A <term>strong water<note>original: "aqua fortis"; a corrosive mineral acid, likely a form of nitric acid, capable of dissolving metals.</note></term> that fixes and makes <term>Mercury<note>original: "Mercurium"; representing the principle of volatility and the "soul" of the metal.</note></term> red, the <term>Elixir<note>The "Philosopher's Stone" in liquid form, used to transform base metals into gold.</note></term>:

Thereafter, put them together into a <term>cucurbit<note>A gourd-shaped glass or ceramic vessel used in distillation.</note></term> that is well <term>luted<note>original: "verlutiert"; sealed with an airtight paste.</note></term>, and place an <term>alembic<note>The "still-head" or cap of the distilling apparatus that collects and directs vapors.</note></term> upon it; seal it well with a <term>lute<note>original: "luto"; a sealant.</note></term> made of rye flour. Set it to distill with a small fire. First, a beautiful, pure, clear water will come forth. Keep that first part separate from the second, and the second from the third; and thus receive and keep—

# The Gift of God.

Keep each [liquid] for itself in a well-closed <term>vessel<note>original: "Gutter"; a narrow-necked bottle or flask used in early chemistry.</note></term> and pay heed: when you see the third water coming out and the <term>alembic<note>The "still-head" or cap of the distilling apparatus that collects the vapors.</note></term> becoming red, then immediately seal the <term>ampoule<note>A small glass vial or container.</note></term> along with the beak of the alembic; for it is a sign that the water has surged or boiled. At that point, the strongest [spirit] comes. And as soon as the alembic begins to turn white, stop giving it fire, and remove the ampoule from there, and seal the <term>receiver<note>original: "receptacul"; the vessel used to collect the distilled liquid.</note></term> with wax, so that it does not evaporate. And of this aforementioned water, take 1 ounce or 2, and put into it 1 ounce of <term>Mercury<note>original: "Mercurij"; quicksilver.</note></term> and 2 ounces of <term>native sulfur<note>original: "lebēdig schwebel"; natural, unrefined sulfur.</note></term>. Put them together into a glass and seal it well, and let it stand thus for 1 hour; then it will be entirely <term>dissolved<note>original: "dissoluiert".</note></term> into a beautiful water. And once it is dissolved, set this <term>solution<note>original: "dissolution".</note></term> with the well-sealed glass into warm ashes and <term>calcine<note>original: "bekall"; to heat a substance to a high temperature but below its melting point to bring about thermal decomposition or eliminate volatile substances.</note></term> it. Then you will find the "dead" Mercury, as red as <term>minium<note>original: "minien"; red lead (lead tetroxide), used here as a color comparison.</note></term> and <term>fixed<note>original: "fix"; in alchemy, a substance that is "fixed" has been made solid or non-volatile, so it no longer evaporates or escapes when heated.</note></term>, so that it in no way [departs] from the fire—

# The Gift of God.

Those, however, who whiten through white things or make white, and yellow through yellow things, while the form of the previous metal remains in the matter, they are deceivers and they do not make true Gold and true Silver. <note>The author warns against "tincturing" or plating metals—simply changing their surface color—rather than achieving a true alchemical transformation of the substance's essence.</note>

The heat that works in the moist creates and brings first the blackness, and in the dry, the whiteness, and in the white, the yellowness. <note>This refers to the traditional stages of the "Great Work": *Nigredo* (blackness), *Albedo* (whiteness), and *Citrinitas* (yellowness).</note>

A <term>strong water<note>original: "aqua, fort"; likely *aqua fortis* or nitric acid, a powerful solvent used to dissolve metals.</note></term> that fixes and makes <term>Mercury<note>original: "Mercurium"; quicksilver.</note></term> red, an <term>Elixir</term>: take <term>saltpeter<note>original: "Salpetre"; potassium nitrate.</note></term> and <term>Roman vitriol<note>original: "Vitrioli Romani"; iron or copper sulfate.</note></term>, one pound of each; crush them and grind them very well.

Thereafter, put them all together into a <term>cucurbit<note>A gourd-shaped glass vessel used as the bottom part of a still.</note></term> well <term>luted<note>original: "verlutiert"; sealed with an airtight paste to prevent the escape of vapors.</note></term>, and put an <term>alembic<note>The "still-head" or cap of the distilling apparatus.</note></term> upon it, and lute it well with a <term>lute<note>original: "luto"; a sealing paste.</note></term> that is made with rye flour. And set it to <term>distill<note>original: "distillieren"; the process of heating a substance to capture its purified vapors.</note></term> with a small fire; then first comes a beautiful, pure, clear water; keep the same for the second, the second for the third, and thus receive and ke—

<meta>catchword: halt (keep)</meta>

<summary>This page critiques fraudulent alchemists who only alter the surface color of metals and provides a specific recipe for creating "strong water" (aqua fortis) through the distillation of saltpeter and vitriol.</summary>
<keywords>alchemy, transmutation, aqua fortis, distillation, deception, Mercury, saltpeter, vitriol, laboratory apparatus</keywords>

# The Gift of God.

**Adeps** <note>original: ".i."; abbreviation for the Latin "id est," meaning "that is"</note> the liquid that is distilled last; the thin substance that is distilled at the very end.

**Adsamar**, that is, washed, or urine <note>original: "lotum, uel lociu, locio, geweschen oder brunz"</note>.

**Adarris** is the "flower of sea water," that is, the foam or salt-bloom of the sea.

**Vinegar of the Philosophers** <note>original: "Acetum Philosophorum"</note>, which is made from fresh sea snails <note>original: "testudinibus marinis"; though "testudo" can mean tortoise, in alchemical distillation it often refers to specific shells or snails</note> through sublimation and distillation, or dripping down.

**Affarx**, that is, <term>atrament<note>original: "atramentum"; a term for various dark, ink-like substances, usually vitriol or iron sulfate.</note></term>; also called *affaris*.

**Affroton**, that is, frothy or foamy.

**Affeos**, that is, foam; *affros* is the same.

**Affreng**, that is, <term>minium<note>A bright orange-red pigment made from lead tetroxide.</note></term>.

**Afragar**, that is, <term>verdigris<note>original: "uiride æris" or "spongrün"; the green crust that forms on copper, used as a pigment and chemical agent.</note></term>.

**Affrodina**, that is, Venus <note>In the symbolic language of alchemy, Venus represents the metal copper.</note>.

**Aycophos**, that is, <term>burnt ore<note>original: "æs ustum"; calcined copper or bronze, used in various medicinal and chemical preparations.</note></term>; *alecofoc* is the same.

**Affronitrum** is the foam of saltpeter <note>original: "spuma nitri"</note>, which in Arabic is called **Baurach** <note>Borax or natron</note>. Or Affronitrum, that is, the salty foam of saltpeter, which is <term>glass-gall<note>original: "glaßgal"; the layer of impurities or "scum" that floats on top of molten glass during its manufacture.</note></term>, as some say.

**Affronitri**, that is, the foam of glass [glass-gall].

**Affronitrum**, that is, salt from Cappadocia <note>A region in Turkey known for its rock salt deposits</note>.

**Affidra**, that is, <term>white lead<note>original: "cerusa" or "Bleyweiß"; a lead-based white pigment widely used in early chemistry and art.</note></term>; also called *allmat*.

**Aphrodisia**, that is, the age of Venus, or lust <note>original: "ætas Venerea"; refers to the reproductive years or the state of sexual desire.</note>.

**Affenicum**, that is, the soul <note>original: "anima"; in alchemy, the "soul" often refers to the volatile or life-giving principle of a substance.</note>.

# The Gift of God.

**Agar**, that is, <term>calx<note>original: ".i. Calx, Kalch"; a powder produced by heating a substance, especially a metal or mineral, to high temperatures; in this context, lime.</note></term>; *algir* is the same, as are *algit* and *algerit*.

**Akibot**, that is, sulfur <note>original: ".i. sulphur, Schwebel"</note>; also called *alchibit* or *alchnit*.

**Alahabar**, *alabari*, *alchonor*, *allarinoch*, *alhohonoch*, *alrachas*, *alastrob*, *alomba*, *alooc*, that is, lead <note>original: ".i. plumbum, Bley"</note>; *allabor* and *alconut* are the same.

**Alafor**, that is, alkali salt; *alafort* is the same.

**Alafreg** is a species of <term>ceruse<note>original: "cerusae" or "Bleysweiß"; white lead, used as a pigment and in chemical processes.</note></term>, it is a type of white lead.

**Alasalet**, that is, <term>sal ammoniac<note>original: "armoniacum"; ammonium chloride, a volatile salt used to clean metals or in the sublimation process.</note></term>.

**Alachaschec**, that is, caltrop <note>original: ".i. tribulus"; a plant with prickly seeds, or a term sometimes used for a spiked chemical vessel.</note>.

**Alahic**; some say it is an alchemist's furnace, while others say it is coal.

**Althanacha**, that is, <term>orpiment<note>original: ".i. auripigmentum"; an arsenic sulfide mineral known for its deep yellow color, used as a pigment and in attempts to create gold.</note></term>; also called *alerner* or *albirnec*.

**Alfatide**, that is, <term>sal ammoniac<note>original: "sal armoniacus, Salmiax"</note></term>; also called *alacap*, *alorap*, *altob*, *alisteles*, *alcob*, *azonec*, *anoxadir*, *anacab*, *andex*, *aquila*, *Butrum*, or *alizeles*.

**Alcol**, that is, vinegar <note>original: ".i. acetum, Essich"; note that "alcol" here refers to vinegar, not modern ethanol.</note>.

**Alcoel**, that is, sour milk <note>original: ".i. acetosum lac".</note>.

**Alchitran**, that is, the dregs of distillation <note>original: "fex distillationis, Trüsen der Distillierung"</note>; it is also called juniper oil <note>original: "oleum iuniperinum, Rackholder öl".</note>.

**Albanum**, that is, salt of urine <note>original: ".i. sal urinae, Harn saltz".</note>.

**Alos**, *alo*, *alix*, *almele*, *alec*, all mean salt; also called *alkalat* or *alkalac*.

**Alga**, <term>grindings<note>original: "Schliff"; the fine sediment or sludge produced when grinding or polishing stones or metals.</note></term>.

**Almene**, that is, clear, shining salt, or <term>rock salt<note>original: "sal lucidum... oder Salgemma"; halite or mineral salt, prized for its purity.</note></term>.

**Alexanthi** or *altingat*, that is, the flower of the ore <note>original: "flos aeris, Blüm / oder Blüst des Ertzes"; the fine flakes or green-blue crust that forms on copper or bronze, similar to verdigris.</note>.

**Alkibric**, **alkibert**, **algibic**, or **alkibic**, that is, native sulfur; "living" sulfur; also called *alchibric*. <note>original: ".i. sulphur viuũ, lebendiger Schwebel"</note>

**Alkin**, that is, <term>potash<note>original: ".i. cinis clauellatus. Weidäschen"; literally "willow ashes," a source of potassium carbonate used as an alkaline agent.</note></term>; also called *alkal*.

**Alima** is a type of sand found within gold-bearing ores, from which lead is produced.

**Alchemy**: from the Greek, [meaning] in English "happiness," or that which makes one joyful and spirited. <note>The author provides a historical folk etymology here, connecting 'alchemy' to the Greek *chairo* (to rejoice) rather than the standard derivation from *chemeia* (the art of alloying metals).</note>

# The Gift of God.

**Alkamia** is the name of a philosopher.

**Alchemy** is a powder artificially made from the <term>basilisk<note>In alchemy, the "basilisk" often symbolizes a highly corrosive substance or the final, transformative stage of the Philosopher's Stone.</note></term>.

**Alkali**, that is, fissile <note>splittable or flaky</note> vitriol, or fusible <note>meltable</note> vitriol.

**Almartack**, that is, <term>litharge<note>original: "litharginus cinis, gletti aschen"; lead oxide residue remaining after the refining of silver.</note></term> ash.

**Alcor**, that is, <term>burnt copper<note>original: "es ustum, brant ertz"; copper oxide produced by heating copper with sulfur or salt.</note></term>, appearing as grains.

**Alzemafor**, that is, cinnabar <note>mercury sulfide</note>.

**Alchitram**, that is, juniper oil or liquid pitch; or prepared arsenic.

**Alkara**, that is, a <term>cucurbit<note>original: "Cucurbita"; a gourd-shaped glass vessel used in distillation as the lower part of an alembic.</note></term>.

**Alembic**, that is, the vessel in which rosewater is made.

**Algali**, that is, nitre <note>saltpeter</note>.

**Algeriae**, that is, lime <note>calx</note>.

**Afronitri**, that is, foam of the sea <note>often referring to meerschaum or a specific salt crust</note>.

**Alusen**, that is, sulfurated <note>treated with sulfur</note>; or *alusinem*, or...

# The Gift of God.

**Altimio**, that is, lead dross <note>original: "fex plumbi, Bley trůsen"; the residue or scum that forms on the surface of molten lead during smelting.</note>.

**Alkoel**, that is, lead extracted from ore <note>original: "plumbum exquisitū de minera, Bley vß der Ertz ersůcht"</note>; some say it is <term>lapis lazuli<note>A deep-blue metamorphic rock used since antiquity as a gemstone and a pigment.</note></term>, while others say it is <term>antimony<note>original: "antimoniū Spießglas"; specifically stibnite, the primary ore of antimony, used in alchemical processes to "purify" gold.</note></term>.

**Alrambus**, that is, a red stone, namely the blood from the veins of men <note>original: "lapis rubeus, Ein roter stein / scilicet sanguis uenarum hominum"</note>.

**Almabri** is a stone similar to amber <note>original: "ambræ"; this could refer to yellow fossilized resin or ambergris.</note>.

**Alhatib** is a red stone <note>original: "lapis rubeus, ein roter stein"</note>.

**Atebras**, or an eagle's hook <note>original: "uncus aquinus"</note>, that is, a <term>sublimation vessel<note>original: "uas sublimatoriū, ein sublimier geschierr"; a laboratory apparatus used for the process of sublimation, where a solid is heated into a gas and then condensed back into a solid form.</note></term>.

**Alefantes**, that is, <term>flower of salt<note>original: "flos salis, Saltzblum oder blůst"; a fine crust of salt that forms on the surface of saline water.</note></term>.

**Algemer**, that is, charcoal <note>original: "carbones Kolen"</note>.

**Almatatica**, that is, raw copper metal <note>original: "metallum cupri, row Kupffer metall"</note>, which is found in the mineral or ore, just like iron metal or ore.

**Alma**, that is, water <note>original: "aqua, wasser"</note>.

**Aludir**, **antaric**, **azomfes**, or **azon**, that is, <term>Mercury<note>original: "Mercurius"; referring to the element quicksilver, considered by alchemists to be one of the three fundamental principles of all matter.</note></term>.

**Alkantum** is a type of <term>atrament<note>original: "atrament"; a term traditionally used for black pigments or vitriols (metallic sulfates) used in making ink.</note></term>.

**Alfasir** or **aluasir**, that is, a shell or a shard <note>original: "testa, ein Schalen / oder scherb"</note>.

**Alatan**, that is, <term>litharge of lead<note>original: "lithargyrum plumbi Bley glet / Hafner glest"; lead monoxide, a secondary product of silver refining often used by potters for glazing.</note></term>.

**Albantina**, a stone of milk salt <note>original: "lapis salis lacti"</note>.

**Almakist** or **almakanda**, that is, litharge <note>original: "lithargirum"</note>.

**Alcore**, a certain stone that has layers resembling silver <note>original: "quidam lapis habens folia similia argento, ein stein der bletter hat dem silber gleich"; likely referring to mica or a similar foliated mineral.</note>.

# The Gift of God.

**Jewish bitumen** <note>original: "men Iudaicum, Jüdisch bäch". Also known as bitumen of Judea or Syrian asphalt, used in ancient medicine and embalming.</note>.
**Asagi**, that is, **red atrament** <note>original: "atramentum rubeum, rot atrament". Atrament was a general term for various metal sulfates used as inks and dyes.</note>.
**Asub**, that is, the **galaxy** <note>original: "galaxia".</note>, the coloration or purification of the stars.
**Asingar** or **asugar**, **asmiar**, that is, **verdigris** <note>original: "viride eris spongrun asigi". Literally "green of copper"; a green pigment formed by the action of acetic acid on copper plates.</note>.
**Asenec**, that is, the **sun** <note>original: "sol". In alchemical symbolism, the sun represents gold.</note>.
**Asabon**, that is, **soap** <note>original: "sapo".</note>.
**Afamar**, **flower of copper** <note>original: "flos æris". The fine oxide that flakes off heated copper.</note>.
**Asop**, that is, **soot** <note>original: "fuligo ruß".</note>.
**Asedenigi**, that is, **hematite** <note>original: "emathites". A reddish-black mineral form of iron oxide, often called bloodstone.</note>.
**Asep**, **aseb**, that is, **alum** <note>original: "alumen". A crystalline substance used as a mordant in dyeing and as an astringent.</note>.
**Asagen**, that is, **dragon’s blood** <note>original: "sanguis draconis, Drackenblüt". A bright red resin obtained from certain plants, used as a pigment and medicine.</note>.
**Aslanegi**, **asanirgi**, **asaragi**, the powder that falls from the **walls of salt** <note>original: "das puluer so da falt vō den wenden des saltzs".</note>.
**Asagi**, **azegi**, that is, **vitriol** <note>original: "vitriolum". A group of hydrated metal sulfates, often used in alchemy to create acids.</note>.
**Ased** in Arabic / **Leo** in Latin, **Lion** in English <note>original: "Löw teütsch".</note>.
**Afuoli**, that is, **atrament**, also called **soot** <note>original: "atramentum, dicitur etiam fuligo, Es heyßt auch ruß".</note>.
**Assaltos** or **asphaltos** in Greek, in Latin **Asphaltum** [Asphalt].
**Azul**, that is, **lapis lazuli**, **azure** in Arabic the same, **azure stone** <note>original: "lapis lazuli, lasurd Arabicè idẽ, Lasurstein". A deep-blue metamorphic rock used as a semi-precious stone and pigment.</note>.
**Alubedegi**, is a stone that **splits other stones** <note>original: "est lapis scindens alios lapides, Ein steyn der andre steyn zerspaltet". Likely a reference to diamond or another extremely hard abrasive mineral.</note>.
**Yellow atrament**, that is, **colcothar** <note>original: "Atramentum citrinum .i. colcotar". Iron(III) oxide produced by the calcination of vitriol.</note>.
**Red atrament** is called **asurie** or **asagi**.
**White atrament**, that is, **calcadis** <note>original: "Atramentum album .i. calcadis". Likely white vitriol or zinc sulfate.</note>.
**Green atrament**, that is, **calcandum**, **Roman vitriol** <note>original: "Atramentum uiride .i. calcandum, vitriolum Romanum". Usually iron sulfate used in making ink.</note>.
**Spanish atrament**, I believe it is **vitriol**.
**Atrament**, a type of **duenec**, **vitriol**.
**Atrament**, that is, **malagillaca**, **black chalk** <note>original: "schwartz kreid".</note>.

# The Gift of God.

**Greek pitch** <note>original: "Atramentum Krieschbech". Often refers to *Atramentum Graecum* or bitumen, used as a black pigment or for its adhesive properties.</note>.
Of the **atraments** <note>A general term for black metallic sulfates, vitriols, or inks.</note>, one is **akata**, another is **alfrein**, another is **kalkadis** <note>A corruption of *chalcanthum*, usually referring to green vitriol or copper sulfate.</note>, another is **calcantum**, and another is **Egyptian atrament**.
**Attingat**, which is **flower of copper** <note>original: "flos æris". The fine oxide or "bloom" that forms on the surface of copper when heated.</note>.
**Atragar**, that is, a **stone**.
**Atureb**, that is, **glass** / **azuzeze**.
**Aurancum**, that is, **eggshells** / **aurantũ**. <note>Used in alchemy as a source of calcium carbonate.</note>
**Attingir**, that is, an **earthen trough**, a small clay vessel.
**Atanor**, that is, a **perforated pot**, a vessel that is pierced with holes through the bottom and the sides. <note>original: "ein Hafen der am Boden vnd zum syten durchlöcheret ist". While "Athanor" usually refers to a specific type of furnace, here it describes a specialized drainage or ventilation pot.</note>
**Atinkar**, that is, **rock borax**, borax from the cliffs. <note>A mineral salt used as a flux in metallurgy.</note>
**Atac**, that is, **talc**, or **nitre**. <note>Nitre refers to saltpeter (potassium nitrate).</note>
**Auuer**, that is, **pure or soft water**, clean or mild water.
**Orichalcum** <note>original: "Auricalcum". A gold-colored alloy, typically brass.</note>, which is spoken of as **Jewish ore**; for *aurum* means **gold** and *calcos* is **Greek** for **ore**.
**Living gold** <note>original: "Aurũ uiuum".</note> is actually **quicksilver** <note>original: "argentũ uiuum". Mercury.</note>, also called **soot-yellow**.
**Red orpiment** } **Realgar** <note>A highly toxic arsenic sulfide mineral used as a red-orange pigment.</note>
**Smelter-smoke**. } <note>original: "Fumus casę / Hüttenrauch". Arsenic trioxide, collected as a powdery byproduct from the flues of smelting furnaces.</note>
**Lemnian orpiment**, **lily water**.
**Fugitive orpiment**, so called because it **flees the fire**. <note>It is volatile and evaporates easily when heated.</note>
**Refined gold**, that is, **gold filings**, or **pure gold**.
**Beaten gold** <note>original: "Aurum batus".</note>, that is, gold in a soft **tincture** or coloring, which tints other bodies through that which is mixed into it.
**Philosophers' gold**, that is, **lead**. <note>original: "plumbum". Alchemists often used the names of precious metals to refer to "base" metals in specific states or stages of the Great Work.</note>
**Cooked gold**, **beaten gold**.

# The Gift of God. 269

**Azegi, azec**, that is, **ink-water** <note>original: "aqua atramenti". Likely a solution of vitriol (metallic sulfates) used for making ink or as a reagent.</note>.
**Azec**, that is, **green vitriol** <note>original: "atramentum uiride". Usually ferrous sulfate.</note>.
**Azagor**, that is, **verdigris** <note>original: "uiride æris". A green pigment formed by the action of acetic acid on copper.</note>, also called **azragar**.
**Auraric**, that is, **mercury** <note>original: "mercur".</note>, also known as **azoch**, **azog**, **azet**, **Besech**, or **besec**.
**Azamar**, that is, **vermilion** <note>original: "vermilío". A bright red pigment made from cinnabar (mercury sulfide).</note>; **azymar** is the same, or **minium** <note>Red lead.</note>.
**Azernec**, that is, **orpiment** <note>original: "alfadida". A yellow arsenic sulfide mineral.</note>.
**Azimar**, that is, **flower of copper** <note>original: "flos æris". The fine oxide that forms on the surface of heated copper.</note>, or **burnt copper**.
**Azub**, in Arabic; in Latin, **alum** <note>original: "alumen". An astringent mineral salt.</note>, also called **azel**.
**Azemafor**, that is, **minium** or **cinnabar**.
**Azedegim**, that is, **hematite** <note>original: "emathites". An iron oxide mineral known as bloodstone.</note>.
**Azuc**, that is, **red coral** <note>original: "corallus rubeus, rot Corallen".</note>.
**Asegen**, that is, **dragon's blood** <note>original: "sanguis draconis". A bright red resin obtained from various plants, used as a pigment and medicine.</note>, also called **azogen**.
**Azurie**, that is, **red vitriol** <note>original: "vitriolum rubeum". Possibly a cobalt or iron sulfate.</note>.
**Azaa**, that is, **magra**, or **red earth** <note>original: "terra rubea rot erdtrich". An iron-rich clay used as a pigment.</note>.
**Azeff**, that is, **feather alum** <note>original: "alumen scissum". A fibrous variety of alum or asbestos.</note>.
**Azins** is a **stone** upon which **salt** grows.
**Azubo**, another **vessel** <note>original: "aliud uas, ein ander gschierr".</note>, also called **Bedogar**.
**Azem**, that is, **clarified butter** <note>original: "butyrum coctum, gsotner Ancken". In alchemy, this could refer to a fatty substance or a specific stage of a process.</note>.
**Azeï**, that is, **ink** <note>original: "atramentum".</note>.
**Azumen**, that is, **weight** <note>original: "pondus, gwicht".</note>, also called **baccatum**.
**Azeg**, that is, **vitriol**, also **azezi** or **azegi**.
**Azensali**, that is, a **black stone** found in **gold**; it is also a **moss** that grows upon the rocks.
**Azamo**, that is, the color **indigo** <note>original: "color Indus".</note>.
**Aseph**, that is, **feather alum**.
**Azanec**, that is, **sal ammoniac** <note>original: "armoniacus". Ammonium chloride, a crucial reagent in alchemical sublimation.</note>.

**Hammer scale** <note>original: "Battitura æris". The flakes of oxide that fly off metal—specifically copper or iron—when it is hammered.</note>, that is, the **scales of metals** <note>original: "squamma metallorum, der metallen schupen".</note>; called in Greek **lepidos**, in Arabic **cubel**, **tubel**, **rubel**, or **fuligo**, and in German **Hammerschlag**.
