### The Gift of God. 270

**Batitura rami**, that is, **copper scale** <note>original: "squama veneris". The flakes that fly off copper when it is hammered.</note>, **copper strike**, **copper flakes**, or **copper ash**.
**Baiac**, that is, **white lead** <note>original: "cerusa". A white pigment made from lead and vinegar.</note>.
**Bagedia**, that is, a **pound of 12 ounces**, the **apothecary pound** <note>original: "artzney pfund". A standard weight for medicinal measurements.</note>, which contains 12 ounces.
**Bayda**, that is, a **vessel upon which one distills**, a vessel upon or over which one performs distillation.
**Bakiam**, that is, a **bean** <note>original: "faba".</note>.
**Balneum Mariae**, that is, **warm water** <note>original: "aqua calida". Literally "Mary's bath," a water bath used for gentle heating in alchemy and cooking.</note>.
**Barach panis**, that is, **salt-niter** <note>original: "nitrum salis". Likely saltpeter or a naturally occurring nitrate.</note>.
**Based** or **besed**, that is, **coral** <note>original: "corallus Belesis".</note>.
**Barcata**, that is, the **passage of fire**, the path or passage of the fire.
**Baul**, that is, **urine** <note>original: "vrina, harn".</note>.
**Balititera**, that is, **red earth** <note>original: "terra rubea". Often referring to iron-rich soils or clay used in alchemy.</note>.
**Basara**, that is, **seed** <note>original: "semen".</note>.
**Bardadia**, that is, a **pound** <note>original: "libra".</note>.
**Baurac**, that is, **every kind of salt**, every race or type of salt, as some say; however, others distinguish them as: **Baurac, denequat, borago, borax, yritar, angar** <note>A list of various salts or mineral substances related to borax or natron.</note>.
**Baurac**, that is, **borax**.
**Baurac**, that is, **rock salt** <note>original: "sal gemma". Naturally occurring crystalline salt.</note>.
**Baurac**, that is, **whitened litharge** <note>original: "saphirium lithargyrũ albificatum, weiß gmachte glett". Litharge is lead oxide; "whitened" likely refers to a purification process.</note>.
**Baurac** is a type of salt of **Alzedi & Diabesis**, that is, a **shell of mercury** <note>original: "testudo argenti uiui". Likely referring to a crust or "tortoise shell" formed by mercury during a chemical reaction.</note>.
**Baurac**, that is, **glass salt**, **glass dregs**, **glass foam**, or **glass gall** <note>original: "sal uitri, fex uitri, spuma uitri, fel uitri". These are various impurities and by-products skimmed from the surface of molten glass.</note>.
**Baurac**, that is, **tincal** <note>original: "attinckar". A crude form of borax.</note>.
**Baurac**, that is, any kind of **saltiness** <note>original: "falsuginis".</note>, every race—

# Die Gab Gottes. 271

[a] kind of salt / or saltiness and may be understood of every [kind of] salt.
**Baurach acutum**, that is, **cooked salt** <note>original: "sal coctum". Likely a purified or concentrated salt solution.</note>.
**Baurack** is a **plaster** with which the joints are coated, such as those of gold; [it is made] with egg white and flour.
**Baurac** is also **salted niter** <note>original: "nitrum". In this period, "niter" could refer to saltpeter, soda, or borax.</note>, Armenian / or sulfurous <note>original: "schwäbelisch". Likely referring to a sulfur-bearing variety of mineral salt.</note>.
**Baurac** is made from the **scum of glass** / or of niter <note>original: "schau des glaß". This refers to the impurities or "glass-gall" skimmed off the surface during the glass-making process.</note>.
**Batitura æris**, that is, **copper scale** <note>original: "cubeletus". The metal flakes or "strike" produced when hammering copper.</note>.
**Beelifis**, that is, **Katanos**.
**Belefon**, that is, **balsam** <note>original: "balsamus".</note>.
**Bezar** or **befar**, that is, a **green stone** <note>original: "lapis uiridis". While "bezoar" usually refers to a medicinal mass found in animal stomachs, here it is identified specifically as a green mineral.</note>.
**Berrionis**, that is, **colophony** <note>original: "colofonia".</note>, that is, **pine resin** <note>original: "gummi pini".</note>.
**Bernix**, **varnish**, **sandarac** <note>original: "classa". A term often used for resins used in varnish, such as sandarac or amber.</note>, **juniper gum** is the same; and **bernix** is a thing made from **linseed oil** and **sandarac**, with which one makes colors shine and strengthens them; **Varnish**.
**Berna**, that is, a **glazed vessel** <note>original: "uas uitreatum".</note>, a glazed vessel / **Birmina**.
**Bereos**, that is, **round** <note>original: "rotundum".</note>.
**Biladen**, that is, **steel** <note>original: "calips uel calybs".</note>.
**Bodagi**, that is, **another vessel** <note>original: "aliud uas".</note>.
**Blicare**, that is, **prepared brazilwood** <note>original: "præsil præparatum". A red dye extracted from the wood of tropical trees.</note>.
**Braricia**, that is, **glass** <note>original: "vitrum".</note>.
**Burina**, that is, **pitch** <note>original: "pix".</note>.
**Bolus armenus**, **carpenters' earth** <note>original: "terra carpentariorum".</note>, **red chalk** <note>original: "Rötelstein". Armenian bole is a red, fatty clay used both as a pigment and as a medicine to stop bleeding.</note>.
**Brase**, that is, **coals** <note>original: "carbones".</note>.
**Brumati**, an **earthen glazed vessel** like a **canister** <note>original: "büchs".</note>.
**Botamum**, that is, **burnt lead** <note>original: "plumbum ustum". Lead oxide produced by heating the metal to a high temperature.</note>.
**Borades**, that is, **filings** <note>original: "limatura".</note>.

# The Gift of God. 272

**Bodid**, that is, an **egg**.
**Borax**, the **halter of gold** <note>original: "capistrum auri". Borax was called this because it "rein-in" or held the gold during soldering or refining.</note>, called **tinckar** in Arabic.

> **Crucible** /
> **Junco**, made for pouring.

**Borax** is a **gum** <note>The author uses "gum" loosely here to mean a resinous-looking mineral or flux.</note> with which gold and silver are **consolidated** <note>joined or soldered.</note>, and it is called **natural attinckar**; however, there is another kind that is **artificial**, which is made by art; this [artificial kind] is better for the [alchemical] art than the natural kind.

# The Gift of God. 274

**Caldar**, that is, **tin** <note>original: "stagnum" (stannum).</note>.
**Calmet**, **cosmec**, that is, **antimony**; **cosmet** and **casmet** are the same.
**Calcination of bodies** is a burning of the bodies with strong fire and salt, so that their **sulfurous nature** <note>original: "schwäbliſchheit". In alchemy, sulfur represented the principle of flammability and volatility that needed to be removed to purify a metal.</note> is eradicated.
**Calcitheos**, that is, **verdigris** <note>original: "ærugo ęris", literally "copper rust".</note>, the rust of the metal, or its purification.
**Calcithos**, that is, **green copper**.
**Calcitari**, that is, **alkali salt**; **calcad** is the same.
**Calcitheos**, **calciteosa**, that is, **litharge** <note>original: "lithargyrium". A mineral form of lead(II) oxide produced during the refining of silver.</note>.
**Calcata**, that is, **yellow atrament** <note>original: "atramentum citrinum". A yellow variety of vitriol or metallic sulfate used for dyes and inks.</note>.
**Calusa cyprus**, **Crystal spedres** is the same.
**Calufax** is the **oil of India**.
**Calcaton**, that is, **arsenic lozenges** <note>original: "trochiſci de arſenico". "Trochisci" were small tablets or cakes of material.</note>.
**Calcadis**, that is, **white vitriol** <note>Zinc sulfate.</note>; it is also called **alkali salt**.
**Calcitheos**, that is, **marcasite** <note>A mineral often confused with iron pyrite, used in the production of sulfuric acid.</note>.
**Cali**, that is, **potash** <note>original: "Weydaſch". An alkaline salt obtained by leaching the ashes of burnt plant material.</note>.
**Calamina**, that is, **calamine stone** <note>The primary ore of zinc, essential for making brass.</note>, also called **Galmy**.
**Capistrum auri** <note>Literally "the gold-halter" or "gold-muzzle," so-called because it binds or helps solder gold.</note>, that is, **borax**.
**Cubil**, that is, **red earth** or red soil.
**Cocilio**, that is, a weight of **11 ounces**.
**Comisdi**, that is, **gum arabic**.
**Cassibor**, that is, **coriander**.
**Colofonia**, that is, **Greek pitch** <note>original: "pix græca". Rosin, the solid resin obtained from pines.</note>, pine gum, or resin.
**Black chalk** <note>original: "Creta nigra".</note>, that is, **black sulfur**.
**Capitellum**, that is, **soap-water** or **lye** <note>original: "lixiuiu-um". A caustic alkaline solution.</note>.
**Culatum**, that is, **calcined** <note>Reduced to a dry powder through the action of intense heat.</note>.

# The Gift of God. 275

**Catrobil**, that is, **earth**, **soil**, or **ground**.
**Cinis clavellatus** <note>original: "alkali". Literally "clubbed ashes," this refers to potash or potassium carbonate derived from the ashes of burnt wood.</note>.
**Carsia**, that is, **salt water** <note>original: "aqua salis".</note>.
**Catma**, that is, **gold filings** <note>original: "limatura auri".</note>.
**Coral**, also known as **belisis**.
**Pig’s onion**, that is, **squill** or **sea onion** <note>original: "Cepa porci .i. ſquilla Meerzibel". A plant used in historical medicine and alchemy.</note>.
**Chalchos** in Greek, **aes** in Latin, **ore** in English <note>original: "Ertz teutſch". Generally refers to copper or its alloys like bronze.</note>.
**Copper**, **ore**, **Venus**, and **Cancer** are the same thing <note>In alchemical symbolism, the planet Venus and the zodiac sign Cancer were often associated with the metal copper.</note>.
**Cuperosa**, that is, **burnt copper** <note>original: "æs uſtum". This often refers to copper sulfate or green vitriol.</note>.
**Cathimia**, meaning **silver foam** <note>original: "Silberſchaum". Likely litharge or the dross skimmed off during the refining of silver.</note>.
**Cathimia of iron**, that is, **iron scale** <note>original: "Sindelſtein". The flaky residue or slag produced when iron is forged.</note>.
**Cinericium**, which is a **goldsmith's cupel** <note>original: "Goldſchmidſcherb". A small, porous bowl made of bone ash used to separate precious metals from base metals through intense heat.</note> in which gold or silver is refined.
**Iron cacia**, that is, an **iron spoon** <note>original: "cochlear ferreum".</note> or a **casting ladle**.
**Cymolia** is a whitish substance or **white earth**, and is called **marl** <note>A lime-rich clay often used as a cleaning agent or in medicine.</note>.
**Ram’s brain**, which is **water made from egg whites** <note>original: "Cerebrum arietis". This is a "deckname" (cover name) used to hide common ingredients from the uninitiated.</note>.
**To wax** <note>original: "Cerare .i. corporare, uel miſcere".</note>, that is, to incorporate or to mix.
**To cement**, that is, to compound or join things together in the manner of a cement.
**Heart**, meaning **fire** or **extremely great heat**.
**Cerober**, meaning **water**.
**Ox’s brain**, that is, **burnt tartar** <note>original: "Cerebrum bouis .i. tartarum combuſtum". Burnt potassium bitartrate, a common flux in metalworking.</note>.
**Concha**, that is, a **pot** or **vessel** <note>original: "olla ein Hafen".</note>.
**Celestial Cancer** or **Cinnabar** <note>original: "cynobrium".</note> is **dragon's blood** <note>original: "ſanguis draconis". In this context, it refers to red mercury sulfide or a red plant resin used as a pigment.</note>.
**Cardanum**, which is **garden saffron** or its seed.
**Cantacon**, that is, **garden saffron**.
**Cyrocnia** or **crocomma**, that is, the **dregs of oil and saffron**.
**Cymolea** or **chymolea**, meaning **grindings** <note>original: "Schliff". The metallic dust or sludge produced when grinding tools or metals on a stone.</note>.

->### The Gift of God. 276<-

**Chymus**, that is, a **mass**.
**Cor**, that is, **fire**.
**Canutum**, that is, a **stalk** or **reed**; "cannutum" is the same: a **tube** or **spool**.
**Carmiti**, that is, an **obolus**, a **halfpenny** <note>original: "Häller". A small copper coin of very low value, used here likely to indicate a specific weight or quantity.</note>.
**Cœnum**, that is, **dung** or **manure**.
**Cynnia**, **cymia**, or **carora**, that is, a **vessel similar to a urinal** <note>In an alchemical context, a "urinal" refers to a rounded glass flask with a long neck, used for observing reactions or for distillation.</note>.
**Blind Cucurbit** <note>original: "Cucurbita cęca". A "cucurbit" is a gourd-shaped vessel used in distillation.</note>. This is a vessel or a pot in which the dissolutions or "un-bindings" of salts or other things are combined with vapor or permeated by it.
**Ivy ash** <note>original: "Cinis hederę".</note>, that is, **potash** <note>original: "cinis clauellatus weydaschen". Literally "clubbed ashes," these are alkaline salts harvested from burnt wood, used as a powerful cleaning agent or solvent.</note>.
**Canze**, **carnit**, **canna**, and **cusanum**: various types of vessels.
**Cibation** <note>From the Latin *cibatio*, meaning "feeding." This refers to the process of "feeding" a substance with liquid to maintain its growth or transformation.</note> is as much as **ceration** in bodies; a mixing in its proper place.
**Ceration** <note>From the Latin *cera*, meaning "wax."</note> is a refining or subtilization of the parts, so that their virtue and power may be poured out and spread into the bodies and the moisture which is necessary in the Art of completion <note>The "Art of completion" refers to the final stages of the alchemical Great Work.</note>. Such ceration occurs only through grinding and sprinkling, so that it becomes like a wax that begins to melt or soften at a tempered heat; and note that such ceration, or "waxing," was entirely discovered by the masters of the present day.
**Conum**, that is, **lukewarm** <note>original: "Law".</note>.
**Capillus**, that is, the **stone of Rebis** <note>The "Rebis" (from Latin *res bina*, meaning double-matter) is a central alchemical symbol of the "divine hermaphrodite," representing the perfect union of opposites (spirit and matter, male and female).</note>.
**Camphor** is a **gum from a tree**.

<meta>This page continues the alphabetical glossary of alchemical and mineralogical terms from page 277. # The Gift of God.

**Colcocul**, that is, **mountain minium**. <note>original: "minium montanum, Bergminien". This refers to a red mineral pigment, often associated with cinnabar or red lead.</note>
**Cathimia** is the **soot** or **smoke** that attaches itself to the upper walls during the burning of **roasted copper ore**. <note>original: "æris usti des branten Ertzes". In historical metallurgy, this refers to the zinc oxide or other metallic sublimates that collect in a furnace chimney.</note>
**Cathimia**, that is, **gold**. <note>original: "aurum Gold"</note>
**Cathimia** is a **vein of the earth** from which gold or silver is taken, according to the sayings of several masters: **Cathimia** grows in gold or silver mines. Likewise, **Cathimia assidia**, that is, **silver-cathimia**, which has the color of **litharge** (that is, burnt lead). <note>original: "Lithargyrij, das ist verbranten Bleys"</note>
**Climia eseps**, that is, **cathimia of orpiment**. <note>original: "cathimia auripigmenti". Orpiment is a bright yellow arsenic sulfide mineral used as a pigment.</note> It also has a glittering color or **azure**, and is similar to the cathimia of silver; but **cathimia** taken simply on its own signifies **silver chalk**. <note>original: "cretam argenti, das were Silberkreiden"</note>

->### D<-

**Danic**, that is, a **weight of 6 barleycorns**. <note>original: "pondus 6. granorum hordei". This refers to the *dāniq*, a small unit of weight used in the medieval Islamic world and adopted by European alchemists.</note>
**Dabat**, that is, **mistletoe** or **birdlime**. <note>original: "uiscus". In alchemy, "viscus" often refers to a sticky or glutinous substance.</note>
**Daram**, that is, a **gemstone**. <note>original: "gemma"</note>
**Dansir** or **densir**, that is, **sand**. <note>original: "arena, Sand"</note>
**Debessis**, that is, a **snail**. <note>original: "testudo ein schnegk". While "testudo" often means tortoise, the German "schnegk" (Schnecke) indicates a snail.</note>
**Daib**, **deheb**, **deheheb**, or **deab**, that is, **gold**. <note>original: "aurum Gold". These are transliterations of the Arabic word for gold, *dhahab*.</note>
**Denodatio** (**untying**), that is, **dissolution** (**releasing**). <note>original: "entknüpffung .i. dissolutio auffentlösung". This refers to the alchemical process of breaking down a substance into its liquid or essential form.</note>
**Dem** or **dehin**, that is, **human blood**. <note>original: "sanguis hominis, menschen blut"</note>
**Dehen** in Arabic: This is **any and every kind of oil**, except for **olive oil**; that specifically is called **Zaic** or **zaich**. <note>original: "Baumöl" (tree oil). In early modern German, olive oil was commonly called "Baumöl" to distinguish it from animal fats or mineral oils.</note>

# The Gift of God.

**Dehenez, duenec, duhenec**, that is, **Roman vitriol**; <note>original: "vitriolum Romanum". Roman vitriol typically refers to a high-quality blue vitriol or copper sulfate.</note> **Dehenes**, that is, **ink** or **black pigment**. <note>original: "atramentum"</note>
**Dragantum** is a **gum**; some claim it is **vitriol**. <note>The text notes a confusion between "Tragacanth" gum and "Dragantum" (a name sometimes used for vitriol or copperas).</note>
**Digegi**, that is, a **hen** or **chicken**. <note>original: "gallina ein Henn oder Hůn". In alchemy, the "hen" sometimes refers to a specific type of furnace or vessel, or the "brooding" process of heat.</note>
**Dragantum vitriol**, which is called **Zegi**. <note>Zegi or Sigi is an Arabic-derived term for vitriol.</note>
**Dracacium**, that is, **Saturn**. <note>In alchemical symbolism, Saturn is the secret name for Lead.</note>
**Dikalegi**, that is, **tin**; **ditalem**, **dicalegi**. <note>original: "stagnum Zin". Tin was associated with the planet Jupiter.</note>
**Dragantum**, that is, **Spanish vitriol**. <note>original: "vitriolum Hispanię"</note>
**Denequat**, that is, **borax**.
**Derquet**, that is, **varnish**. <note>original: "vernix"</note>
**Dyamassien**, that is, **flower of copper**, **delsatiteos**. <note>original: "flos æris". This refers to the oxidized flakes or "verdigris" that form on the surface of heated copper.</note>
**Dehene**, that is, **blood**. <note>original: "sanguis Blůt"</note>
**Duenez, doeneck**, that is, **iron filings**. <note>original: "limatura ferri, Eyſenfyleten / oder feyel"</note>
**Duenes**, that is, **ink** or **black pigment**. <note>original: "atramentum"</note>
**Duamir** is a species of **snake** with which one makes **theriac**. <note>original: "Triax". Theriac was a famous ancient panacea; the best versions required the flesh of vipers to "cure" the poison of others.</note>
**Decimar** is **lead** in the nature of **tin**.

**The Two Brothers** <note>original: "Duo fratres, die zween Brüder". This term refers to pairs of metals or principles that are closely related or "born" of the same mineral vein.</note>
*   **Saturn and Jupiter** <note>Lead and Tin</note>
*   **Venus and Mars** <note>Copper and Iron</note>
*   **Gold and silver** <note>original: "Aurum & argentum"</note>

**The Two Brothers** are **edar** and **michati**. <note>These are likely transliterated Arabic terms for specific mineral spirits or metallic states.</note>
**Digestion** means a change and **transformation** of one thing into another, through the **ebullition** and **cooking** of nature. <note>In alchemy, digestion is the process of applying gentle, consistent heat to a substance—often in a "pelican" vessel—to mature it, mimicking the stomach's role in the body.</note>
**Dragantum**: there are four types of it: **Indian**, **Arabian** which is yellow, **Cyprian** which is green...

# The Gift of God.

...is / the fourth is **French earth** <note>original: "terra Francisca"</note>, that is / **black vitriol** <note>original: "schwarz arrament," from "atramentum," usually referring to a black ink or a vitriolic salt used as a pigment.</note>.

### E.

**Elemptis**, that is, the **Sun**, **Ezeph**.
**Edez**, that is, **gold**; **electrum** is the same.
**Edic, edich, ebdanic**, that is, **Mars**. <note>"Mars" is the alchemical name for iron.</note>

**Ematires**, that is, **bloodstone** <note>original: "lapis sanguineus, Blutstein." Likely hematite, an iron oxide.</note>; it is found where one digs for **sulfur**. <note>original: "schwäbel."</note>

**Ema**, that is, **blood**. <note>From the Greek "haema."</note>
**Elixir** or **elei**, that is, **medicine**, a **remedy**.
**Ezezich**, that is, **salt**.
**Effides**, that is, **lead ash**, **lead dust** / or **ceruse**, or **white lead**. <note>original: "cinis plumbi, Bleyasch / uel ceruusa, oder Bleyweiß." White lead was a common pigment and alchemical ingredient.</note>
**Elaquir**, that is, **green vitriol**. <note>original: "vitriolum uiride, grüner vitriel." This is iron(II) sulfate.</note>
**Elepodatum**, that is, **filed** or **filings**. <note>original: "limatum, gefieler."</note>
**Epatum**, that is, **hepatic aloes**. <note>original: "aloë epaticum." A dark, liver-colored variety of aloes used in medicine.</note>

# The Gift of God.

**Elz**, that is, **flower of copper** <note>original: "flos æris," the green oxidation or verdigris that forms on copper surfaces.</note>.
**Elopitinum**, **tragacanth** <note>original: "dragantum," a natural gum derived from the sap of legumes, used as a binder.</note>, **vitriol**.
**Epar**, that is, **air**, or **ether**, the **heavens**, or the **highest element**.
**Aes appodiatum**, that is, **filed metal** or **filings** <note>original: "limatum, gefielet Ertz."</note>.
**Elidrium**, that is, **gum**, **mastic** <note>A resin obtained from the mastic tree.</note>.
**Epofilinga**, that is, **iron scales** <note>original: "squamma ferri, Eysenschůp," the flaky residue produced when iron is forged.</note>.
**Entale** is a **vessel** or a **container**.
**Elidrium** is a **confection of gold and silver**, which is made from two parts **silver**, and one part **gold**, and one part **copper** <note>This refers to a specific alloy or "mixture" used in alchemical operations.</note>.
**Edes**, that is, **gold**, **Elempnis**.
**Elzimar**, that is, **flower of copper**.
**Elanula** is a **hard alum**, like **iron** <note>Alum is a mineral salt used as a mordant and in various chemical processes.</note>.
**Elixir**, or **xir**, is a **penetrating and transformative medicine** <note>original: "durchtringendt vnd vergstaltenden Artzney."</note>, and it is made from **vegetables** <note>original: "vegetabilibus." In alchemy, "vegetable" often referred to substances that could "grow" or change, rather than just plants.</note>, that is, from **four spirits** <note>The "four spirits" typically referred to mercury, sulfur, arsenic, and sal ammoniac.</note>, with the addition and joining of a **body**, which **body** is a **ferment of a medicine**.
**Elersna**, the **processing** or **handling of silver**, or a **vein of silver**, or a **vein of lead**, a **silver lode**, and it is called **molybdena** <note>Historically, "molybdena" referred to various lead-like minerals and ores.</note>.
**Embula** is a **vessel like a tube**; according to the words of a **philosopher**, it is as much as a **cane** or **reed**, or a **pipe** <note>original: "canna" or "fistula."</note>.

->F.<-

**Felilech**, **faules**, that is, **iron** <note>original: "ferrum, Eysen falex."</note>.

# The Gift of God.

**Hara**, that is, **juniper** <note>original: "iuniperus, Räckholder."</note>.
**Hafacium**, that is, **sal ammoniac** <note>original: "sal armoniacum, Salmiax." Ammonium chloride, frequently used as a flux in alchemical refining.</note>.
**Harmat**, that is, the **fruit of the juniper**, or **juniper berry** <note>original: "fructus Iuniperi, Räckholderbeer."</note>.
**Hager**, that is, a **stone** <note>original: "lapis."</note>.
**Hidus**, that is, the **flower of copper** <note>original: "flos æris." A term for the green oxidation or verdigris that forms on the surface of copper.</note>.
**Hadid**, that is, **iron** <note>original: "ferrum, Eysen."</note>.
**Halimar**, that is, **copper** <note>original: "cuprum."</note>.
**Hunc**, that is, **Jupiter**, **tin**, or **hucci** <note>original: "Iupiter, stannum, uel hucci, Zin." In the alchemical system of correspondences, the planet Jupiter is always associated with the metal tin.</note>.
**Helnesed**, that is, **coral** <note>original: "corallus."</note>.
**Holsebon**, **helsaton**, or **hesebon**, that is, **prepared common salt** <note>original: "sal commune præparatum, bereyt gemein Saltz." This refers to table salt that has been purified for chemical use.</note>.
**Spanish green** or **Hyspalensis**, that is, **green of copper** or **verdigris** <note>original: "Hispanicū uiride, Hyspalensis .i. viride æris spansgrün." A bright green pigment and medicinal caustic made by exposing copper to vinegar fumes.</note>.
**Helie**, that is, **mistletoe** <note>original: "viscus." This can refer to the plant itself or the sticky "birdlime" rendered from its berries.</note>.
**Helunhai** is **Solomon's ring** in the **art of necromancy**, it is a **finger ring of Solomon** in the **black arts** <note>original: "annulus Salomonis in arte nigromanciæ, ist ein fingerring Salomonis inn der schwarzen Kunst." Referring to the legendary magical rings used to bind spirits.</note>.
**Hismat**, that is, **silver dross** or **litharge** <note>original: "spuma argenti, Silberschaum." Literally "silver-foam," the metallic ash or scum produced during the cupellation of silver.</note>.

->I.<-

**Idam**, that is, a **porridge** or **pulp** <note>original: "pulmentum, ein Müß."</note>.
**Iffides**, that is, **ceruse** or **lead ash**, also called **white lead** <note>original: "cerusa uel cinis plumbi, Bleyweiß / oder Bleyäsch."</note>.
**Idroagira**, that is, **alkali water** <note>original: "aqua alkali."</note>.
**Idroagiros**, that is, to **set and place under a manure pile** or **dung** <note>original: "vnder ein Mistgüllen / oder Mist setzen vnd thun." An alchemical method of "digestion" where the natural heat of decomposing manure provides a steady, gentle temperature for a vessel.</note>.
**Algir fire**, that is, the **strongest fire** <note>original: "ignis algir .i. ignis fortissimus, aller sterckest fewr."</note>.
**Elemental fire**, that is, **sulfur**, but **not that of the common people** <note>original: "sulphur, sed non vulgi, schwäbel / aber nit des gemeinen volcks." This refers to "Philosophical Sulfur," one of the prime principles of matter in alchemy, rather than common brimstone.</note>.
**Fire of the wise**, that is, **warm horse manure** <note>original: "ignis sapientum .i. fimus equinus calidus, warmer..." Referred to as a "fire" because it provides consistent heat without a flame.</note.

# The Gift of God.

...more **horse manure** <note>original: "roßmist."</note>.
**Brightly burning fire**, that is, **sulfur** <note>original: "Ignis clarè ardens .i. sulphur."</note>.
**Extinguished fire**, that is, **quenched sulfur** <note>original: "Ignis extinctus .i. sulphur extinctus gelöschter schwäbel."</note>.
**Fire**, according to the opinion of some, is the **oil** that floats atop the **distillation**.
**Ios**, that is, **poison** <note>original: "venenum Gifft."</note>.
**Iumnisum**, or **iumnizum**, that is, **ferment**, **sourdough**, or **leaven** <note>original: "fermentum Hebel/ sawrteig/ teisam."</note>.
**Jupiter**, that is, **tin** <note>original: "stagnum, Zin." In alchemy, the planet Jupiter is the symbolic counterpart to the metal tin.</note>.
**Indian salt**, that is, **rock salt** <note>original: "Indicum sal .i. sal gemma Indisch saltz."</note>.
**Indian color**, the color of **woad-blue** <note>original: "Indicus color, weytenblaw farb." This likely refers to indigo or a similar deep blue dye.</note>.
**Imbibing**, that is, **thickening by grinding**, as in the book of **Alexander**; it means making it thick by rubbing, as in [the works of] Alexander. To **imbibe** is to **soak in** <note>original: "Imbibere .i. terendo inspissare, ut in Alexandro... imbiberen eintrinchen." "Alexander" likely refers to the Secretum Secretorum, a text attributed to Alexander the Great.</note>.

->K.<-

**Kayl**, that is, **sour milk** or **buttermilk** <note>original: "lac acetosum, sawre Milch/ kabic."</note>.
**Kal**, that is, **salt of the torrent** <note>original: "sal de torente." Likely salt gathered from dried stream beds or a specific mineral salt.</note>.
**Karabe**, that is, a **gum** like **amber**; it is **amber** <note>original: "gumma sicut safforata, ist Agstein." Agstein is an archaic German term for amber.</note>.
**Kali**, that is, **potash** or the ash called **alkali** <note>original: "cinis clauellatus Weydäschen / oder die äsch die da heißt alkali." Potash was traditionally made by leaching wood ashes (Weydäschen) in a pot.</note>.
**Kaprili**, that is, **sulfur**.
**Kar**, that is, a **gemstone that shines like fire** <note>original: "gemma lucens ut ignis ein Edelgestein / das da leuchtet wie fewr." Likely referring to a carbuncle or ruby.</note>.

--- Page 336 ---
<meta>This page continues the alphabetical glossary of alchemical and medicinal terms from the previous page. It concludes the section on the letter "K" and begins the letter "L." The content focuses on the identification of mineral substances, plant extracts, and their various names across Arabic, Greek, and Latin traditions.</meta>

# The Gift of God.

...the union.

**Kymolea** is the **mud** <note>original: "lutum"</note> that gathers under the grinding wheel or whetstone where weapons are sharpened or polished; it is also called **chymolea**.

**Kali** is a salty herb or plant. When burned, its ashes clump together into a mass; in our language, this is called **soda** <note>original: "seda"</note>, from which glass is made. However, the salt that flows out during the boiling of the glassmaking process is called **alkali salt** <note>original: "sal alkali"</note>.

In **Arabic**, **Kali** is called **ulnen** <note>likely referring to *ushnan*, a name for soda-ash plants</note>. **Rhazes** <note>original: "Rafis," referring to the Persian physician and alchemist Abu Bakr al-Razi</note>, however, says that **kali** is **Assur alum** <note>original: "alumen assur"</note>.

**Katimia** is called **kadmia** in Greek, but in common Latin, it is **calamina** or **calamine stone** <note>original: "lapis calaminaris"</note>. It is a source of **Tutty** <note>original: "Tutie," an impure zinc oxide</note>. More truthfully, it is the raw Tutty itself, for it is the stone with which ore is colored and dyed into **brass** <note>original: "mösch," a variant of Messing</note>. In this process, that which clings to the upper walls of the furnace is the true **Tutty**, which is called **pompholyx** <note>original: "ponpholix," or "white nothing," a byproduct of smelting zinc</note>.

### L.

**Lanx**, that is, **bitter almond** <note>original: "amygdala amara"</note>.
**Poppy milk** <note>original: "Lac papaueris"</note>, that is, **opium**.
**Lacune**, that is, **sealed earth** <note>original: "terra sigillata," a medicinal clay stamped with a seal for authenticity</note>.
**Brick** <note>original: "Later"</note> or **robber** <note>original: "latro"</note>, that is, **quicksilver** <note>original: "argentum viuum," mercury</note>.
**Lamac**, that is, **gum arabic** <note>original: "Gummi Arabicum"</note>.
**Lans**, that is, **dead silver** <note>original: "argentum mortuum"</note>.
**Stone of the mountains** <note>original: "Lapis de montanis"</note>, that is, the **turtle** <note>original: "testudo"</note> or **Rebis** <note>The "two-thing," a central alchemical symbol for the union of spirit and matter</note>.
**Lamare** or **lamne**, that is, **sulfur** <note>original: "sulphur"</note>.
**Lites**, that is, **calamine stone** <note>original: "lapis calaminaris"</note> or **brass stone** <note>original: "lapis aurichalci"</note>.

<summary>This page provides a glossary of alchemical and chemical terms, defining substances like soda, alkali, and Tutty (zinc oxide) and their uses in glassmaking and metallurgy. It also begins a list of terms under the letter "L," including medicinal substances and alchemical metaphors like the "Rebis."</summary>
<keywords>alkali, soda, glassmaking, Tutty, calamine, brass, opium, mercury, sulfur, Rebis, Rhazes, medicinal chemistry</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the alphabetical glossary of alchemical and medicinal substances. It follows the entry for "Lacune" (sealed earth) and covers various mineral, metallic, and organic materials beginning with the letter "L," providing their Latin synonyms, common German names, and alchemical metaphors.</meta>

# The Gift of God.

**Calamine stone** <note>original: "Lapis calaminaris"</note>, that is, **Tutty** <note>original: "Tutia"</note>, according to Master Peter.
**Lime stone** <note>original: "Lapis calcis"</note>, that is, **copper dross** <note>original: "scoria æris"</note> or **copper flakes** <note>original: "batitura æris"; "Kupffertrüfen" or "Kupfferschlag"</note>.
**Lime stones** <note>original: "Lapides calcis"</note>, that is, **iron** <note>original: "ferrum"; "Eyfen"</note>.
**Lazarat**, that is, **lazurium** or **azure** <note>original: "lazurium lasurium"; "Lasur," referring to lapis lazuli or the blue pigment derived from it</note>.
**The fugitive thief** <note>original: "Latro fugitiuus"</note>, that is, **mercury** <note>original: "mercurius"</note>.
**Lanna**, that is, **orpiment** <note>original: "auripigmentum," a yellow arsenic sulfide mineral</note>.
**Wood of the cross** <note>original: "Lignum crucis"</note>, that is, **oak mistletoe** <note>original: "Eichenmistel"</note>.
**Litharge** <note>original: "Lithargyrium"</note> or **almarach**, which is the **ash of any metal**.
**Laos**, that is, **tin** <note>original: "stagnum"</note>.
**Armenian mud** <note>original: "Lutum Armenicum"</note>, that is, **bole** <note>original: "bolus," a medicinal clay</note>.
**Lubam** or **luben**, that is, **frankincense** <note>original: "thus"</note>; he refers to the kind from Lebanon.
**Lorici**, that is, **urine** <note>original: "urina"</note>.
**The Green Lion** <note>original: "Leo viridis"</note>, that is, **vitriol** <note>original: "vitriolum," a sulfate of metal</note>.
**The Lion** <note>original: "Leo"</note>, that is, **gold** <note>original: "aurum"</note>.
**The foliated yellow lion** <note>original: "Leo citrinus foliatus"</note>, that is, **orpiment** <note>original: "auripigmentum"</note>.
**Lebeten**, that is, **pouring cauldrons** or **crucibles** <note>original: "Gießtiegel"</note>.
**The Green Lion** <note>original: "Leo viridis"</note> is, according to the opinion of some, **gold** <note>original: "aurum"</note>.
**Lemnian lime** <note>original: "Lempnias calcis"</note>, that is, **copper flakes** <note>original: "batitura æris"; "Kupfferschlag"</note>.
**Leta**, which is a **red color** <note>original: "color rubeus"; "rote farb"</note>.
**Locium**, that is, the **urine of infants** <note>original: "vrina infantium"; "Kinderharn"</note>.
**Leplia**, that is, **arsenic** <note>original: "arsenicum"</note>.
**Lempnias** or **lempnia**, that is, **orpiment** <note>original: "auripigmentum"</note>.
**Lempnia** is an **earth** in which **gold grows**, or it is **gold-dross** <note>original: "Goldtrüsen"</note>.
**Latten** <note>original: "laton"</note>, that is, **brass** <note>original: "auricalcum"; "mösch"</note>.
**Mud of the Mastery** <note>original: "lutum Magisterij"; "Leym der meysterschaft"</note>, that is, made from **chalk** <note>original: "Kreyden"</note> and **egg whites** <note>original: "Eyerklar"</note>.
**The scale of lime** <note>original: "lepos calcis"</note>, that is, **copper dust** <note>original: "puluis æris"</note>.
**Armenian mud** <note>original: "lutum armenum"</note>, that is, **Armenian bole** <note>original: "bolus armenus"</note>.

<meta>This page continues the alphabetical glossary of alchemical and medicinal terms. The top fragment "men vereinigung" is the conclusion of an entry from the previous page, likely referring to the "union" or "combination" of substances. The text moves from the letter 'K' (covering salts and ores) into the letter 'L'.</meta>

# The Gift of God.

...union.
**Kymolea** is the mud or grime <note>original: "lutum"</note> that collects under the grinding wheel or sharpening stone where weapons are sharpened or polished; it is also called **chymolea**.

**Kali** is a salty herb or plant which, when burned, causes its ashes to clump together into a mass. In our language, this is called **soda**, from which glass is made. However, the salt that flows out during the boiling process of glassmaking is **alkali salt** <note>original: "sal alkali"</note>.
**Kali** in Arabic is *ulnen* <note>original: "vlnen"</note>. Rhazes <note>an influential 9th-century Persian physician and alchemist</note>, however, says that **kali** is **Assur alum** <note>original: "alumen assur"</note>.

**Katimia** is called **kadmia** in Greek, but in common Latin it is called **calamina**, or **calamine stone** <note>original: "lapis calaminaris"</note>. It is a root of **Tutty** <note>original: "Tutia," an impure form of zinc oxide</note>. Indeed, to speak more truly, it is the raw Tutty itself, for it is the stone with which ore is colored and tinted into brass. In this process, the material that attaches itself to the upper walls of the furnace is the true **Tutty**, which is called **pompholyx** <note>original: "ponpholix," often called "white nothing" by alchemists, referring to zinc oxide flowers</note>.

->L.<-

**Lanx**, that is, **bitter almond** <note>original: "amygdala amara"</note>.
**Milk of the poppy** <note>original: "Lac papaueris"</note>, that is, **opium**.
**Lacune**, that is, **sealed earth** <note>original: "terra sigillata," a medicinal clay used as an antitoxin</note>.
**Brick** <note>original: "Later"</note> or **thief** <note>original: "latro"</note>, that is, **quicksilver** <note>original: "argentum viuum," mercury</note>.
**Lamac**, that is, **Gum Arabic** <note>original: "Gummi Arabicum"</note>.
**Lans**, that is, **dead silver** <note>original: "argentum mortuum"</note>.
**Stone of the mountains** <note>original: "Lapis de montanis"</note>, that is, the **tortoise** <note>original: "testudo"</note> or **Rebis** <note>an alchemical symbol representing the "two-fold thing," often the union of spirit and matter</note>.
**Lamare** or **lamne**, that is, **sulfur** <note>original: "sulphur"</note>.
**Lites**, that is, **calamine stone** or **brass-stone** <note>original: "lapis aurichalci"</note>.

# The Gift of God.

**The fugitive thief** <note>original: "Latro fugitiuus"</note>, that is, **mercury** <note>alchemists often used "fugitive" to describe mercury's tendency to evaporate or escape when heated</note>.

# The Gift of God.

**Leuz**, that is, **almond** <note>original: "amygdala"</note>.

**Leucasia**, that is, **quicklime** <note>original: "calx uiua"</note>.

**Magra clay** <note>original: "Lutum magra"</note>, that is, **red chalk** <note>original: "rötelstein"</note>.

**The Rebis stone** <note>original: "Lapis rebis"</note>, that is, **tortoise** <note>original: "testudo"; in alchemy, the Rebis often represents the divine hermaphrodite or the union of opposites</note>.

**Luna**, that is, **silver** <note>original: "argentum"</note>.

**Lorus**, that is, **Mercury** <note>original: "Mercurius"</note>.

**Mastic tree** <note>original: "Lentiscus arbor"</note>, that is, **ash tree** <note>original: "Eschenbaum"</note>.

**Limpid**, that is, **pure or purified** <note>original: "purum uel purgatum"</note>; it is clean, purified, or clear.

**Libda**, that is, **a filter or felt** <note>original: "filtrum, filtz"</note>.

**Adir stone** <note>original: "Lapis adir"</note>, that is, **sal ammoniac** <note>original: "sal armoniacum, Salmiac"</note>.

**Golden stone** <note>original: "Lapis aureus"</note>, so is **urine** itself called by the philosophers who revealed it in Latin. However, one author says that the golden stone is **hair**, or that from which the **blood of animals** comes.

**The famous stone** <note>original: "Lapis famosus"</note> or the **precious stone** <note>original: "lapis preciosus"</note>, these are the same **hairs** <note>original: "capelli"</note>; or the precious or famous stone is **salt of urine**.

**Animal stone** <note>original: "Lapis animalis"</note>, a blessed stone, refers to:
- human blood
- an egg

**Philosophical stone**, that is, **elixir**.

**The Greater Stone** <note>original: "Lapis maior"</note>, that is, the **spirits** extracted from the **bodies** <note>in alchemy, "bodies" refers to the base metals</note>.

**The Greater Stone**, according to the opinion of some, is the **calcined and purified bodies**.

**The Greater Stone** is composed of **four elements** and is called the **son of one day** <note>original: "filius unius diei," a term implying the stone is created in a single final step once the materials are ready</note>.

**Jewish stone** <note>original: "Lapis Iudaicus"</note>, **kidney stone** <note>original: "Harnstein"</note>, or the **stone of Agapis**.

**The philosophers' stone**; these are the **hairs of man**.

--- Page 341 ---
<meta>This page continues the alphabetical glossary of alchemical terms and substances under the letter 'L', specifically focusing on different varieties of 'Lapis' (stones). It transitions from the previous page's general definitions into a more detailed list of mineral, animal, and symbolic "stones" used in the Great Work.</meta>

# The Gift of God.

**The stone that is extracted from man** <note>original: "Lapis qui extrahitur ab homine"</note> is the blood of man.

**Hidden stone** <note>original: "Lapis occultus"</note>, **blessed stone** <note>original: "lapis benedictus"</note>, that is, an **egg** <note>original: "ouum"</note>.

**Stone of the moon** <note>original: "Lapis lune"</note>, **selenite** <note>original: "afroselinum"</note>, and **mirror stone** <note>original: "specularis"</note> are the same.

**Iron scales** <note>original: "Lepi ferrei"</note> are the **shavings of iron** <note>original: "squama ferri, eysenschüp"</note>; it is also called **lepidos**.

**Litharge** <note>original: "Lithargyrum uel lithargiros"</note> is **silver dross** <note>original: "spuma argenti"</note> or silver foam.

**Sealed clay** <note>original: "Lutum sigillatum"</note>, that is, **sealed earth** <note>original: "terra sigillata"</note> <note>A medicinal earth, often clay from Lemnos, stamped with a seal to guarantee its authenticity as an antidote to poison</note>.

**Animal stone** <note>original: "Lapis animalis"</note>, that is, the runner that carries a shield on its back <note>original: "curenta quæ scutum portat in dorso"</note>, a **tortoise**.

**Stone of wine** <note>original: "Lapis uini"</note>, that is, **tartar** <note>original: "tartarum"; the sediment or crust found in wine barrels</note>.

**Litharge** <note>original: "Lithargirum"</note>, the stone in which **gold grows**.

**Lemnian earth** <note>original: "Lempnias"</note>: some have interpreted this to be **orpiment** <note>original: "auripigment"; a yellow arsenic sulfide mineral</note>, but this is false; for it is **sealed earth** <note>original: "terra sigillata"</note> that is red in color.

**Hematite stone** <note>original: "Lapis hematitis"</note>, **bloodstone**; so called because its juice <note>original: "lab"</note> is blood-colored, or because it serves to treat all flows of blood.

**The Rebis stone** <note>original: "Lapis rebis"</note> is the **pair** <note>original: "Baar"</note> from choleric or sanguine people <note>In alchemy, the Rebis or "double thing" represents the union of opposing qualities, here linked to the four humors</note>.

**The stone that is not a stone** <note>original: "Lapis non lapis"</note>, that is, the **Elixir**, for Elixir means a great treasure.

**The stone that is not a stone** <note>original: "Lapis non lapis"</note> is called **mercury** <note>original: "mercurius"</note> by some: it is called a **stone** because of its power, but **not a stone** because of its essence. However, **Avicenna** <note>A famous Persian polymath and influential alchemical authority</note> and **Rodar** say that the "stone that is not a stone" is the **Elixir**; it is called a **stone** because it tinges or is colored, and **not a stone** because it is poured <note>i.e., it is liquid or fusible rather than a solid mineral</note>.

<summary>This page provides an alchemical glossary of various "stones," ranging from physical substances like tartar and iron scales to symbolic concepts like the "stone that is not a stone" (the Elixir).</summary>
<keywords>Lapis, Litharge, Terra sigillata, Rebis, Elixir, Avicenna, Hematite, alchemy, metallurgy, symbolism</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the alphabetical glossary of alchemical terms and substances. # The Gift of God.

**Lapis lazuli**, **Azure stone** <note>original: "Lasurstein"</note> is of two kinds: one is so hard that it does not yield to iron, and is of a sky-blue color; this one is commendable. The other is crumbly, of a pretty color, like **azure earth** <note>original: "terra azarina"</note>.

### M.

**Marcellus**, that is, a great hammer <note>original: "malleus magnus"</note>.
**Madic**, that is, the milk remaining after butter; **buttermilk** <note>original: "lac post butyrum / Ancken milch"</note>.
**Manna**, **mamma**, is the substance with which the vessels are glazed.
**Malanter**, that is, **opium**.
**Mellipodium**, that is, **burnt lead** <note>original: "plumbum ustum"</note>.
**Magra**, that is, **red earth** <note>original: "terra rubea, rot erdtrich"</note>.
<margin>Three alchemical and astrological symbols: a circle surmounted by a cross (representing Antimony or a variant of Venus/Copper), the symbol for Jupiter (♃, representing Tin), and the symbol for Mars (♂, representing Iron).</margin>
**Micha**, that is, **Venus** <note>The planet or the metal copper</note>.
**Masellum**, **mosel**, that is, **Jupiter** <note>The planet or the metal tin</note>.
**Mars**, that is, **iron** <note>original: "ferrum Eyfen"</note>.
**Marcasite** <note>original: "Marckasita"</note>, that is, **calcitheos** <note>A term for copper-like minerals or copper pyrites</note>.
**Malthedorum**, that is, **rock salt** <note>original: "sal gemma"</note>.
**Manheb**, that is, **slag** or **cinder-stone** <note>original: "scoria, Sindelstein"</note>.
**Leaden marcasite** <note>original: "Marcasita plumbea"</note>, that is, **antimony**.
**Magra**, that is, **carnelian** <note>original: "carniolus uel carneolus"; a reddish gemstone</note>.
**Maruch**, that is, **oil**; the same applies to metal.
**Magnesia** is a stone with the power of marcasite, or it is a stone similar to **hematite** <note>original: "hemathiti"; an iron oxide mineral</note>. Likewise, **magnesia**, that is, **woman** <note>original: "foemina Weib"</note>; magnesia is also **bismuth** <note>original: "Wyßmat"</note> or **barren ore** <note>original: "taub Ertz"; literally "deaf" ore that contains no valuable metal</note>.
**Marcasite**, that is, **droplets of the ore** <note>original: "trüfen des Ertzes"</note>; just as **cadmia** <note>original: "climia"</note> is the droplets of gold, and **antimony** is the droplets of lead.
**Masculine** <note>original: "Masculus"</note>, that is, **quicksilver** <note>original: "argentum uiuum"; mercury</note>.
**Magnesia**, that is, a **tortoise** <note>original: "testudo"</note> or **sulfur**.
**White marcasite** <note>original: "Marcasita alba"</note>, is called **silver pyrite** <note>original: "Silberkiß"</note> or **bismuth**.

<term>Nitrum</term>, that is, <insert>
<term>Soap</term>.
<term>Talc</term>.
</insert>
<term>Nysadir</term>, <term>nussiadai</term>, <term>nestudar</term>, that is, <term>sal ammoniac</term>. <term>Salmiar</term>.
<term>Nigella</term>, that is, <term>corn cockle</term> <note>original: "Ratten"; a common weed, though the Latin "Nigella" usually refers to black cumin</note>.
<term>Nitrum</term> is a type of salt. It is also <term>borax</term>, and there are several types of <term>Armenian stone</term> <note>original: "armeni"; likely lapis armenus, a blue carbonate of copper</note>: some black, some red, and some sapphire-colored.
<term>Nitrum</term> is a <term>false stone</term>, a salted stone, that is, <term>white sulfur</term>.
<term>Nitrum Alexandrinum</term>, that is, <term>potash</term> <note>original: "cinis clauellatus", "Weyßäsch"; literally "clove-ash," used in making soap and glass</note>.

# The Gift of God.

<term>Egg</term> <note>original: "Ouum"</note>, that is, the <term>blessed stone</term> <note>original: "lapis benedictus", a common synonym for the Philosopher's Stone</note>.
<term>Ozeman</term>, that is, <term>egg white</term> <note>original: "album ouorum"</note>, white from eggs.
<term>Orogama</term>, that is, <term>gold</term> <note>original: "aurum"</note>.
<term>Osotor</term>, that is, <term>opium</term>.
<term>Obelchara</term>, that is, a <term>cucurbit</term> <note>original: "cucurbita", a gourd-shaped chemical vessel used in distillation</note>.
<term>Ops metallim</term>, that is, <term>quicksilver</term> <note>original: "argentum viuum", literally "living silver" or mercury</note>.
<term>Opilare</term>, that is, to <term>cover</term> <note>original: "operire"</note> or to <term>clog</term> <note>original: "verstopffen"</note>.
<term>Ochre</term>, yellow earth that painters use, and is called <term>ochre</term> <note>original: "Ocra"</note>.
<term>Blessed oil</term> <note>original: "Oleum benedictum"</note>, is made with bricks <note>also known as "Oil of Bricks," made by soaking bricks in oil and distilling them at high heat; used for joint pain and alchemical dissolving</note>.
<term>Oil of grain</term> <note>original: "Oleum frumenti"</note>, oil of spelt grains is made in two ways: namely, with a glowing metal plate, and through descending distillation.
<term>Oil of sulfur</term> <note>original: "Oleum sulphuris"</note>, these are sublimated or raised mists from prepared sulfur.
<term>Oil</term> is also the name given to the first water that distills from <term>blood</term> <note>original: "sanguine"</note>, or from <term>hair</term> <note>original: "capillis"</note>.
Likewise, <term>Oil</term> and <term>water</term> distill together, but the oil floats on top. Likewise, <term>oil</term>, that is, <term>fire</term> <note>original: "ignis"</note>, which is extracted from a dry substance; but <term>water</term> is from a moist substance; and then, that which remains at the bottom of the vessel is called <term>pure earth</term> <note>original: "terra pura"</note>, clean earth.

->P.<-

<term>Father before the Son</term> <note>original: "Pater ante Filium"</note>, the Father before the Son, that is, the <term>slaying of the father</term> <note>original: "interfectio patris"</note>, that is, <term>Satyrion</term> <note>an orchid plant, though in alchemy this often refers to a specific stage where a "base" material is destroyed to produce the "son" or Stone</note>.
<term>Father</term> <note>original: "Pater"</note>, that is, <term>sulfur</term>.
<term>Pelicide</term>, that is, <term>cooked honey</term> <note>original: "mel coctum"</note>, also called <term>Pelude</term>.

# The Gift of God.

<term>Penetration</term> <note>original: "Penetratio"</note>, that is, <term>submerging</term> <note>original: "submersio"</note>: a piercing through or sinking down.
<term>Bloodstone</term> <note>original: "Petra sanguinaria"</note>, that is, <term>hematite</term> <note>an iron oxide mineral often used in alchemy and medicine for its blood-red streak</note>.
<term>Pegernus</term> or <term>the pilgrim</term> <note>original: "peregrinus"</note>, that is, <term>mercury</term> <note>original: "mercurius"</note>.
<term>Pegernus</term> is also lime made from the whitest parts <note>literally "ears"</note> of sea fish.
<term>Locust’s foot</term> <note>original: "Pes lucuste"</note> or <term>bruste foot</term>, that is, <term>Jamenese alum</term> <note>original: "alumen iameni", a variety of alum likely named after Yemen</note>.
<term>Pistare</term>, that is, to <term>crush</term> <note>original: "contundere"</note> or pound.
<term>Pestle</term> <note>original: "Pistillum" or "pistrillum"</note>, that is, a pestle or the club used with a mortar.
<term>Pisare</term>, that is, to <term>strain</term> <note>original: "colare"</note>: to filter through a cloth or otherwise.
<term>Phibit</term>, that is, <term>predatory</term> <note>original: "rapax"</note> or snatching.
<term>Green leek-stone</term> <note>original: "Prasium uiride"</note>, that is, <term>flower of copper</term> <note>original: "flos æris", referring to verdigris or copper acetate</note>.
<term>Psincus</term> or <term>psinckis</term>, that is, <term>white lead</term> <note>original: "cerusa", a white pigment produced by exposing lead to vinegar</note>.
<term>Porfiligon</term>, that is, <term>iron-scale</term> <note>original: "batitura ferri"</note>: the hammer-slag that flies off iron.
<term>Poponac</term>, that is, a <term>gum</term>, a specific resin called by this name <note>likely Opopanax, used in incense and medicine</note>.
<term>Porphyreticum</term>, that is, a mortar made of bronze or copper, or a grinding stone.
<term>Poctus</term>: a burning vessel <note>a container used for heating substances</note>.
<term>Phanlec</term>, that is, <term>iron</term> <note>original: "ferrum"</note>.
<term>Pompholyx</term>, <term>spodium</term>, and <term>tutty</term> <note>original: "Ponpholix & spodium, & tutia"</note> are all one and the same <note>different names for zinc oxide residue collected from smelting furnaces</note>.
<term>Patias</term>, that is, <term>scale</term> <note>original: "squama"</note> or metal flakes; <term>lepida</term> means the same.
<term>Pilos</term>, that is, <term>clay</term> <note>original: "argilla"</note>, loam, or <term>moist earth</term> <note>original: "terra uimida"</note>, fresh soil.
<term>Pyrides</term>, called <term>stone</term> <note>original: "lithos"</note> in Greek: <term>marcasite</term> <note>a brittle mineral often confused with pyrite or "fool's gold"</note>.
<term>Pixari</term>, meaning <term>pitch</term> <note>original: "picem"</note> or tar.
<term>Psamos</term>, from the Greek, means <term>sand</term> <note>original: "arena"</note>.
<term>Plithos</term>, from the Greek, means <term>cold</term> <note>original: "frigus"</note>.
<term>Liquid pitch</term> <note>original: "Pix liquida"</note>, turpentine, or <term>Glorian</term> <note>a name for pine resin</note>.

->Q.<-

<term>Quebricum</term> for <term>Kebrit</term> <note>the Arabic term for sulfur</note>. Some say it is <term>arsenic</term> <note>original: "arsenicum"</note> and should be written with a "K," but Stephanus says it is <term>sulfur</term> <note>original: "Sulphur"</note>.

# The Gift of God.

<term>Egg</term> <note>original: "Ouum"</note>, that is, the <term>blessed stone</term> <note>original: "lapis benedictus," a symbolic term for the Philosopher's Stone</note>.

<term>Grain oil</term> <note>original: "Oleum frumenti"</note>, <term>spelt grain oil</term> is made in two ways: namely, with a glowing metal plate and through <term>descending distillation</term> <note>a method where the substance is heated from above and the liquid flows downward into a receiver</note>.
<term>Oil of sulfur</term> <note>original: "Oleum sulphuris"</note>, these are sublimated or elevated vapors from prepared sulfur.
<term>Oil</term> is also the name given to the "first water" that distills from <term>blood</term> <note>original: "sanguis"</note> or from <term>hair</term> <note>original: "capilli"</note>.

However, <term>water</term> is from a moist substance; and that which then remains at the bottom of the vessel is called <term>pure earth</term> <note>original: "terra pura"</note>.

-># The Gift of God. 297<-

**Penetration** <note>original Latin: "Penetratio," meaning to pierce through</note>, that is, **submersion**, a piercing through, which is to say, a sinking down.
**Blood-stone** <note>original: "Petra sanguinaria"</note>, that is, **hematite** <note>an iron oxide mineral often used in medicine and alchemy to stop bleeding</note>.
**Pegernus**, or the **traveler** <note>original: "peregrinus"</note>, that is, **quicksilver** <note>mercury</note>.
**Pegernus** is also the **lime** <note>calcium oxide or powdered residue</note> made from the very whitest **ears of sea-fish** <note>likely referring to otoliths or specific white shells</note>.
**Locust's foot**, or **brush-foot**, that is, **Yemeni alum** <note>a specific, highly-valued type of alum sourced from Yemen</note>.
**To pound** <note>original: "Pistare"</note>, that is, to **crush** or strike.
**Pestle** <note>original: "Pistillum"</note>, that is, a pounder or a mortar-club.
**To strain** <note>original: "Pisare"</note>, that is, to **filter** through a cloth or otherwise.
**Phibit**, that is, **predatory** or ravenous.
**Green leek-stone** <note>original: "Prasium uiride"</note>, that is, the **flower of copper** <note>verdigris, the green pigment that forms on weathered copper</note>.
**Psincus** or **psinckis**, that is, **white lead** <note>original: "cerusa"</note>.
**Porfiligon**, that is, **iron scales**, the hammer-flakes from iron.
**Poponac**, that is, a **gum** <note>Opopanax</note>, a gum so named.
**Porphyry-tool** <note>original: "Porfireticum"</note>, that is, a **bronze or copper mortar**, or a rubbing plate.
**Poctus**, a **burning-vessel** <note>a container used for distillation or calcination</note>.
**Phanlec**, that is, **iron**.
**Pompholyx**, **spodium**, and **tutia** <note>various forms of zinc oxide collected as dust from furnace flues</note> are all one and the same.
**Patias**, that is, **metal scale** or flake; **lepida** is the same.
**Pilos**, that is, **potter's clay** or loam; **moist earth** <note>original: "terra uimida"</note>, meaning fresh soil.
**Pyrites**, called **stone** <note>original: "lithos"</note> in Greek, which is **marcasite** <note>a generic term for various shiny sulfide minerals</note>.
**Pixari**, that is, **pitch**.
**Psamos** in Greek, that is, **sand**.
**Psithos** in Greek, that is, **coldness**.
**Liquid pitch**, **turpentine**, or **Glorian**.

### Q.

**Quebricum** for **Kebrit** <note>an Arabic-derived term for sulfur</note>; some say it is **arsenic**, and that it should be written with a K, but **Stephanus** <note>likely referring to Stephanus of Alexandria, a famous early alchemist</note> says it is **sulfur**.

# The Gift of God. 298

**Quars** <note>original Latin: *petra fellis*</note>, that is, **gallstone**.
**Quehirt**, that is, **sulfur**; **Quibrith** is the same.
**Quianos**, that is, **vein of the earth** <note>original Latin: *vena terræ*</note>, a vein of the earth, and it is called **copperas** <note>original: *cuperosum*; a term used historically for metallic sulfates like iron or copper vitriol</note>.

->R.<-

**Rastol**, that is, **ore** or bronze <note>original Latin: *æs*</note>; **Rasoes** is the same.
**Rabira** or **radira**, that is, **tin** <note>original Latin: *stannum*</note>.
**Rafaheti**, **rusatagi**, or **rusangi**, that is, **burnt ore** <note>original: *brant Ertz*; roasted metal or copper calcined with sulfur</note>; also known as **Kosange**.
**Rabiel** or **Rosiel**, that is, **dragon’s blood** <note>original Latin: *sanguis draconis*; a bright red resin used as a pigment and in medicinal preparations</note>.
**Radix** means **radish** <note>While the Latin *radix* simply means 'root,' it was commonly used in early modern German to refer specifically to the radish</note>.
**Raibz** or **rus**, that is, **stone** <note>original Latin: *lapis*</note>; **Raeb** is the same.
**Rastul**, **salt**.
**Racri**, that is, **sal ammoniac** <note>original Latin: *sal armoniacus*; ammonium chloride, a vital salt used in alchemical sublimation and as a flux</note>; also **Raan** or **ranac**.
**Ramigi**, that is, **colophony** <note>original Latin: *colofonia*; a form of pine resin</note>, or **ramigiri**, **reddish-yellow** <note>original German: *rotigenig*</note>.
**Rasas** or **rastis**, that is, **tin** <note>original Latin: *stannum*</note>.
**Ramad**, that is, **ashes** <note>original Latin: *cinis*</note>.
**Rachi** or **racho**, that is, **mercury** <note>the liquid metal quicksilver</note>.
**Rainale**, a stone from which **cinnabar** <note>original: *vzifer*, a corruption of *uzufur*, an Arabic-derived term for cinnabar or vermilion</note> is made.
**Rinar**, in Greek, means **filings** <note>original Latin: *limatura*; the fine dust produced by filing metal</note> or iron-filing debris.
**Rel** or **rebur**, that is, **acidic** <note>original Latin: *acetosum*</note> or **sour milk**.
**Recha**, **marble**.
**Rebis**, that is, the **stone of Rebis**, namely **hair** <note>original Latin: *capilli*; while *Rebis* usually refers to the alchemical hermaphrodite, in this glossary tradition it is frequently a code word for human hair, which was sometimes distilled for its nitrogenous content</note>.
**Robes**, that is, **vinegar** <note>original Latin: *acetum*</note>; **roscod** is the same; and as some claim, **robes** is **Saturn** <note>the alchemical name for the metal lead</note>.
**Realgar**, that is, **red orpiment** <note>original Latin: *auripigmentum rubeum*; a red arsenic sulfide mineral</note>, or **arsenic sulfide** <note>German: *Rußgell*</note>.
**Rotumha**, a **vessel** similar to a **cucurbit** <note>a gourd-shaped flask used as the lower part of a distillation apparatus</note>.
**Rub** is **thickened juice** <note>original Latin: *succus inspissatus*; an Arabic-derived term for a syrup or extract boiled down to a thick consistency</note>.
**Rosa**, that is, **tartar** <note>original Latin: *tartarum*; the potassium bitartrate crust that forms inside wine barrels</note>.
**Ron**, that is, a **pound** <note>original Latin: *libra*; a unit of weight</note>; **roula** is the same.

# The Gift of God. 299

->S.<-

**White salt** <note>original Latin: *Sal albus*</note>, that is, **borax**.
**Salt allocaph**, that is, **sal ammoniac** <note>original Latin: *sal armoniacus*; ammonium chloride, often used as a flux in refining metals</note>.
**Salt alkali**, that is, [salt] of bitterness <note>original Latin: *amaritudinis*</note>, **dregs of bitterness** <note>original German: *Drüsen* (likely *Drusen*) *der Bitterkeyt*; referring to the sediment or alkaline residues</note>.
**Bread salt** <note>original Latin: *Sal panis*</note>, that is, **common salt not prepared**, **common salt that is not prepared**.
**Usual salt** } 
**Mass salt**  } that is, **bread salt**, **bread salt**.
**Chaste salt** <note>original Latin: *Sal castus*</note>, that is, **common salt prepared**, **common salt that is prepared**.

**Indian salt**, that is, **mercury** that has been **dissolved** and **coagulated**.
**Salt alabrot**, that is, **black nitre**, **black saltpeter**.
**Salt alabrot** is made from the juice of three herbs and is called **the master of all things**; it works this wonder: that it transforms **mercury** into true **Luna** <note>silver</note>, or into **Sol** <note>gold</note>; it is also called **Salboroch**.

**Sal ammoniac**, that is, **salt of Saparicia**, or **dug [salt]**, or **salt from Arabia**.

-># The Gift of God.<-

**Sandix**, that is:
* **Wild cabbage** <note>original Latin: *olus agreste*</note>, a wild cabbage plant named by the Greeks.

**Foam of nitre** <note>original Latin: *spuma nitri*</note>, **saltpeter-foam**; in Greek **aphronitron**, in Arabic **borax** <note>original: *baurach*</note>.

**Tumbil**, that is: **earth**.
**Tumpaba**, **timbapar**, **capapiru**, **thion**, that is: **native sulfur** <note>original Latin: *sulphur vivum*</note>.
**Titanos**, that is: **lime made from gypsum**.
**Trigias** in Greek, that is: **tartar**; **Trigia** is the **dregs** or **lees** <note>original German: *Trusen*</note>.
**Tubel** is a **flake** or **hammer scale** <note>original German: *Hammerschlag*</note> of any metal that falls from the anvil, and in Greek it is called **lepidos**.
**Tumbalum**, that is: **tubel** <note>referring to hammer scale, as noted above</note>.
**Tipsaria**, that is: **barley water**, and it is called **ptisan** <note>a medicinal infusion or decoction</note>.
**Tutty** <note>zinc oxide residue</note>, that is: **calamine**; look under **K** for **cadmia** <note>original German: *Katimia*</note>.
**Targir**, that is: **almond oil**.

V.

**Vas fictile**, that is: a **potter’s vessel made of clay**.

<meta>catchword: Vas</meta>

<summary>This page concludes the "T" section of an alchemical glossary, defining various mineral earths, sulfur types, and metal byproducts like hammer scale. It emphasizes the practical identification of substances used in both medicine and metallurgy.</summary>

<keywords>terra sigillata, orpiment, sulfur, borax, hammer scale, tartar, tutty, calamine, almond oil, alchemical terminology</keywords>

--- Page 359 ---
<meta>This page continues the alphabetical glossary of alchemical terms, transitioning from the end of the letter "T" (noted in the previous page) to the start of the letter "V." It focuses on metaphors for volatile substances and common mineral preparations.</meta>

-># The Gift of God.<-

**Raw vessel** <note>original Latin: *vas crudum*</note>, i.e., **new**, a new or raw vessel.
**Vein of Venus** <note>original Latin: *vena veneris*</note>, i.e., **vervain** <note>original Latin: *verbena*; a herb often associated with the planet Venus in medical alchemy</note>.
**Valrat**, i.e., **leaf** <note>original Latin: *folium*</note>; **vlrat** is the same.
**Varach**, i.e., **dragon's blood** <note>original Latin: *sanguis draconis*; a bright red plant resin used as a pigment and medicine</note>.
**Red wind** <note>original Latin: *ventus rubeus*</note>, i.e., **red orpiment** <note>original Latin: *auripigmentum rubeum*; likely referring to realgar, a red arsenic sulfide</note>.
**Yellow wind** <note>original Latin: *ventus citrinus*</note>, i.e., **sulfur**.
**Ulsa** or **lapsatura**, i.e., **grindings** <note>original German: *Schlieff*; the waste dust produced by grinding or polishing metal or stone</note>.
**White wind** <note>original Latin: *ventus albus*</note>, i.e., **mercury** <note>original Latin: *mercurius*</note>.
**Winds**: the **winds** are called **spirits** <note>original Latin: *spiritus*</note>, which are the **spirits** in this art <note>in alchemy, "spirits" refers to volatile substances that can be sublimated or distilled, such as sulfur, mercury, and ammonia</note>.
**Horse’s belly** <note>original Latin: *venter equi*</note>, i.e., **horse dung** <note>original Latin: *fimus equinus*; frequently used as a source of gentle, steady heat for digestion or fermentation</note>.
**Venus**, **copper** <note>original Latin: *cuprum*</note>; **murpuc** is the same.
**Vener**, i.e., **Saint James’s shells** <note>original German: *Sant Jacobs Muschlen*; scallop shells, used both as laboratory vessels and as symbols of pilgrimage</note>.
**Utrum**, i.e., **the whole egg** <note>original Latin: *totum ouum*; often a metaphor for the alchemical vessel or the "Philosophical Egg"</note>.
**Verdigris of copper** <note>original Latin: *utride æris*</note>, **Greek green**, **Spanish green**, **copper rust** <note>original Latin: *ærugo æris*</note>, i.e., **verdigris** <note>original German: *Spangrün*; the green pigment formed by the weathering of copper</note>.
**Vermiculum**, **little worms**, **elixir**, **tincture**.
**Uzifur**, i.e., **cinnabar** <note>original Latin: *cynobrium*; mercury sulfide</note>; **uzufar** is the same.
**Usrub**, **ursub**, **usurub**, **uzurup**, i.e., **Saturn** <note>the alchemical name for lead</note>.
**Ulisur**: some say it is **minium** <note>red lead</note>; **vafur** is the same.
**Vermilion**, i.e., **cinnabar** or **minium**, or it may refer to any red color.
**Vernice**, i.e., **gummy varnish**, **dry varnish** <note>original German: *Truckner Virniß*</note>.
**Ussidz**, i.e., **dross of gold** <note>original Latin: *scoria auri*</note>, or **gold purification**.
**Roman vitriol** <note>original Latin: *vitriolum romanum*</note>, i.e., **green atrament** <note>original Latin: *atramentum uiride*; green vitriol or ferrous sulfate</note>.
**White vitriol** <note>original Latin: *vitriolum album*</note> is the same.

<summary>This page provides a glossary of alchemical terms starting with the letter 'V,' defining various substances like verdigris, cinnabar, and vitriol. It notably explains the use of "winds" as a metaphor for volatile spirits and "horse's belly" as a term for dung-based heating.</summary>
<keywords>Venus, Saturn, vitriol, cinnabar, mercury, spirits, alchemy, verdigris, dragon's blood, orpiment, glossary</keywords>

--- Page 360 ---
<meta>This page continues the alphabetical glossary of alchemical and medicinal terms, moving from the letter V (concluding the previous page) through the final letters of the alphabet: X, Y, and Z. The text focuses on mineral pigments, various names for mercury, and botanical substances used in the "Great Work."</meta>

-># The Gift of God.<-

### X.

**Xiston**, that is, scraped copper rust <note>original Latin: *ærugo rasa*; original German: *Abgeschabner Erzroßt*; likely referring to verdigris or copper acetate</note>.

### Y.

**Ydragyros**, that is, quicksilver <note>original Latin: *argentum viuum*; mercury</note>.
**Yris**, that is, iron <note>original Latin: *ferrum*; original German: *Eyßen*</note>.
**Ysir**, that is, any color; **yspar** is the same.
**Ygropissos**, that is, bitumen or pitch <note>original German: *Bäch*</note>; it is said <note>original German: *sonat es tönt*</note> to be liquid pitch <note>original Latin: *pix liquida*</note>, which is thin pitch.
**Ydroteum**, **ydracium**, **ydens**, **ydrargyros**, that is, quicksilver <note>original Latin: *argentum viuum*</note>.
**Yarin**, that is, flower of copper <note>original Latin: *flos æris*; refers to the green or blue oxidation on copper surfaces</note>; **yaria** and **yarin** are the same.
**Yelion** and **yalos** in Greek, that is, glass <note>original Latin: *uitrum*; original German: *Glas*</note>.
**Yc**, **yo**, that is, good <note>original Latin: *bonus*; original German: *Gut*</note>.
**Yxir**, that is, a good medicine <note>original Latin: *bona medicina*; original German: *ein gute Artzney*; a variant of the word "elixir"</note>.
**Yridis** or **yride**, that is, orpiment <note>original Latin: *auripigmentum*; a yellow arsenic sulfide mineral</note>.
**Yomo** and **yos**, or **yn** in Greek, that is, verdigris <note>original Latin: *uiride æris*; original German: *Spangrün*</note>.

### Z.

**Zaphyra**, that is, Oriental saffron <note>original Latin: *crocus orientalis*; original German: *Orientischer Saffran*</note>.
**Zarneck**, that is, orpiment <note>original Latin: *auripigmentum*</note>; **zarnec**, **zarnech**, and **zarne** are the same.
**Zatanea**, that is, the flower of the Chaste Tree <note>original Latin: *flos agni casti*</note>; **Zuccaiar** is the same.
**Zaybac**, **zeida**, **zaibach**, **zaibar**, **zerachar**, **zibatum**, that is, quicksilver <note>original Latin: *argentum viuum*</note>.
**Zafran**, that is, garden saffron <note>original Latin: *crocus hortulanus*; original German: *Gartensaffran*</note>.
**Zarnich**, that is, arsenic.
**Zaras**, that is, gold <note>original Latin: *aurum*</note>; **zomir** is the same.
**Zamafor**, that is, minium <note>original German: *minien*; red lead pigment</note>.

<meta>catchword: Saffron</meta>

<summary>This page concludes the alphabetical alchemical glossary with the letters X, Y, and Z, providing synonyms for key materials like quicksilver (mercury), orpiment, copper rust, and saffron. It reflects the multilingual nature of alchemical study, drawing from Greek, Latin, German, and Arabic-derived terms.</summary>

<meta>This page concludes the alchemical glossary titled "The Gift of God," specifically finishing the section for the letter Z. # The Gift of God.

**Zaffron**, that is, ochre <note>original Latin: *ocra* or *ocrea*; original German: *ouger* (Ocker); a natural earth pigment</note>.
**Zaffaben**, that is, a well-drop <note>original Latin: *putea gutta*; likely referring to a specific liquid distillation or spring water</note>.
**Zarfa**, that is, tin <note>original Latin: *stangnum* (stannum)</note>.
**Zancel**, that is, the yew tree <note>original Latin: *taxus*; original German: *yben* or *yen* (Eiben)</note>.
**Zub**, that is, raw butter <note>original Latin: *butyrum crudum*; original German: *roher Butter*; also known as *zubd*</note>.
**Zymar** or **zynsar**, that is, verdigris <note>original Latin: *uiride æris*; original German: *Spangrün*; copper acetate used as a green pigment</note>.
**Zegi**, **zet**, or **zezi**, that is, vitriol <note>original Latin: *vitriolum*; a family of metallic sulfate minerals central to alchemical practice</note>.
**Zoaco**, that is, Venus <note>In alchemical symbolism, Venus is the planetary correspondence for copper</note>.
**Zericum**, that is, arsenic.
**Zeo**, that is, I boil <note>original Latin: *ferueo*; original German: *Ich seud* (siede)</note>.
**Zeherech** or **alchas**, that is, flower of copper <note>original Latin: *flos æris*; the oxidation or "bloom" found on copper surfaces</note>.
**Zengar**, that is, verdigris or flower of copper.
**Zoraba**, that is, vitriol.
**Zeitrabra**, that is, flowable <note>original Latin: *fluxibile*; original German: *flüssig*; refers to a substance that melts or flows easily</note>.
**Zebeb**, that is, dung <note>original Latin: *stercus*; original German: *Dräck* (Dreck)</note>; **chara** is the same.
**Zebd**, that is, butter <note>original Latin: *butyrum*; original German: *Ancken* (Anken, a regional term for butter)</note>.
**Zefr**, that is, pitch <note>original Latin: *pix*; original German: *Bäch* (Pech)</note>.
**Zec**, that is, tragacanth <note>original Latin: *dragantum*; a natural gum used as a binder</note>.
**Zemech**, that is, lapis lazuli <note>original German: *Lasurstein*; a deep blue stone used to create ultramarine pigment</note>.
**Zerefari**, that is, whey <note>original Latin: *aqua casei* (cheese water); original German: *Käßmilch* or *Käßwasser*</note>.
**Zengifur** or **zemafarum**, that is, cinnabar <note>original Latin: *cynobrium*; the primary ore of mercury, used as a bright red pigment</note>.

->F I N I S.<-

->Samuel Apiarius.<-
